## Handoff: RT-33 raw 0x2e random-movie control runtime (2026-10-16 PDT, cloud thread)

Scope: the brief asked for raw 0x2e and 0xd1 in one slice; 0xd1 is the Turning Puzzle (a phase machine with its own process-list callback, surface load, sound and timing; the size of RT-24's Mouselight puzzle), so this slice lands 0x2e alone and 0xd1 is the next family. Type-level scenes with every row handled: 1,946 to 1,954 of 2,078 (upper bound; the row-level live count needs the Mac; RT-32's 0x43/0x94 will add to it when it merges).

What 0x2e is: dispatcher arm `0x427f62` (gate mode 0), handler `0x4ced84` on the random-movie object `0x63821d` (its debug text is `RANDOMMOVIE_ProcessPlayRandomMovie`). Every visit stores the row and the signed body byte in the object; only if the object is active (`+0x216` nonzero) does the mode act (1 = variable store, scene change, start flag, state `+0x1de := 2`, `+0x206 := 0`; 2 = toggles `+0x1de`/`+0x206`/`+0x25a`; 0 = the random-movie stop `0x4ceb3b`); then the completion byte of the row at `+0x242` is set directly (DIRECT_COMPLETED, phase untouched, whatever the subtype). `+0x216` is written 1 only by raw 0x2d (`0x4cd7f4`), which is not bound, so in the running game the object is inactive and a 0x2e row stores the row and mode and completes. Corpus: 10 rows in 10 scenes, modes 0 (6), 1 (2), 2 (2).

Merged in this slice: `src/session_bindings_a2e.[ch]` (the object fields live in the binding as `SessionA2EMovie`, for the future 0x2d binding to share; start-flag sink and transition request needed only on the active mode-1 path; active mode 0 is UNSUPPORTED before any effect), registration `bindings[25]` in `blackmoor_app.mm` (count 26), `scripts/build_action_family_2e_runtime.sh`, modules added to `build_native_app.sh`, packet `tasks/RT-33-random-movie-2e-runtime.md`.

Verification: 9 C unit cases (ASan/UBSan clean), 17 unit mutants and an executor-level case with one mutant; corpus run of the 10 rows inactive and active; original-x86 oracle `tests/action_family_2e_runtime_original_oracle.py`: the real dispatcher `0x427a50` and handler `0x4ced84` run 534 scenarios, 648 passes, 4,951 ordered stores compared (object, event flags, start flag, scene-change cells, completion bytes), 52 passes ended at the active mode-0 stop where the native binding refused the same row, 0 mismatches; 36 of 36 native mutants caught. The full suite (compared with plain main) and the independent review are recorded in the PR description.

Not verified: the macOS glue was not compiled or run (needs the Mac). Active mode 0 (`0x4ceb3b` and the movie-set release it triggers through `0x4cd218`) and everything raw 0x2d does to the object are not modelled (the app object stays inactive); in the original the stop leaves `+0x242 = -1`, so the handler's own completion store lands at row -1 and the 0x2e row is revisited, which belongs with 0x2d. The scene loader `0x40b34a` was not run (rows are built in memory); the frame-step readers of the object (`0x4383ed`, `0x4394fb`) are host behaviour. Ledger: no new acceptance (231 ported); evidence pins re-pinned only.

Next: (1) raw 0xd1 Turning Puzzle (11 scenes, object `0x61d449`, handler `0x4b64a9`; callback `0x4b7051`; see the packet's last section). (2) raw 0x2d as the owner of the random-movie object, then active 0x2e mode 0. (3) Mac check of the glue.

For a future raw 0x2d / mode-0 owner: `0x4ceb3b` also sets the completion byte of the row in obj+0x23e (`0x4ceb75..0x4ceb7f`), and the movie state in `SessionA2EMovie` needs a reset hook once 0x2d lands.

## Handoff: Track M tables slice, switch tables and int 3 padding rebuilt from source (2026-10-20 PDT, cloud thread)

Result: the composer no longer places a single synthesized byte. The last **8,070 structured bytes** (172 switch jump tables 5,856, 381 `int 3` runs 2,214) now come from assembler sources: **158 tables (5,616 B) and all `int 3` padding** from the already audited function `.s` files (`SWTAB_<va>`, `PAD_<va>`), and the **240 B** of 14 tables between the code runs of the two CRT block-copy routines (`0x0050ed50`, `0x0050fa90`; the fragment generator cuts those spans at the tables, so no function source carries them) from a new audited data batch `asm_data/data-005` (profile `jump_tables`, 12 ranges, `.long CODE_<va>` case addresses). `compose_image.py build`: structured 0 B, reconstructed 1,511,424 B, referenced 0, missing 0, composed SHA-256 equals the original. That is composer evidence, **not a whole-file x86 match claim** and not "game complete" (native gameplay is open).

What changed:
- `compose_image.py`: audited switch tables and the `int 3` padding a structured group also describes are bound (class `tool_assisted_reconstructed_jump_table` for tables; padding stays `tool_assisted_reconstructed_padding`) and replace the structured items (`replaces_structured`, the existing `supersede_structured` tiling check). The structured groups are still generated and compared with the original first (`structured_mismatches`, CLI exits 1 on a mismatch), so the descriptions remain a cross-check. The coverage key `structured_replaced_by_data_batches` is renamed `structured_replaced_by_audited_sources` (1,039 items, 31,500 B: 23,430 headers/imports/resources from batches 003/004, 5,856 tables, 2,214 `int 3`).
- `data_asm.py`: profile `jump_tables`, `table_ranges()` (the dword tables of `text_data.json` that no audited function carries, adjacent ones merged), `gen --jump-tables --batch 005`; the auditor requires the manifest to claim exactly `table_ranges()`. Batches 001 to 004 regenerate unchanged.
- `decomp-progress/generate.py`: a `jump_tables` batch is validated (inside `.text`, hashes, audit) but adds **no** rows, because those bytes sit in code atoms the map already accounts for. **Map accounting is unchanged**: code 1,201,488 of 1,213,018, data 298,406 of 298,406. The 11,530 bytes under the code bar (tables 5,856, `int 3` 2,214, index tables 1,934, alignment padding 1,526) are now all assembler-sourced, but still sit outside the matched-code bar by design; moving them would change both totals (11,530 bytes from code to data), which I did not do.
- Docs: `image/README.md`, `asm/README.md`. `AGENTS.md` is not edited: data-005 uses the existing ruling (1) form (jump tables as `.long` addresses) through the data generator, with no separate owner ruling; the pending card wording about headers is untouched. **For Glib to know:** batch 005 puts 240 bytes of code-section jump-table addresses in the data class path; if that is not wanted, drop `data-005` from `image/reconstructed.json` and those 240 bytes fall back to structured generation (a test proves it).

What is left before a whole-file x86 match could be claimed (all still true after this slice):
1. **No single build.** Each function is assembled as its own object at an `.org` and the Python composer concatenates 1,480 verified ranges by file offset. Nothing assembles and links the pieces into one PE, so there is no source-to-exe build whose output equals the original.
2. **The repo cannot rebuild on its own.** Data (`.rdata`, `.data`, resources, headers, the 240 B tables) is regenerated from the owner's local `GAME.EXE` at build time under ruling (6) and never committed; the code is committed as mnemonics.
3. **14 bytes are compiled, not assembled:** the two incomplete C ranges (`src/initial_functions.c`, clang, class `incomplete_byte_range_not_a_complete_function`) win over batch 007's assembly for the same bytes. Dropping that binding would fix it but changes the baseline accounting (398 matched bytes), so I left it.
4. **Provenance and review.** 4,090 functions carry machine annotation only (names derived from bytes, no recovered intent, branch conditions uninterpreted); no original C++ or compiler provenance is claimed; the 22 fragments count only bound with their successor (ruling 5); the headers/import tables count under ruling (6) only pending Glib's card.
5. **Not in the file:** the uninitialized `.data` tail (virtual size only) is not a composed byte and not claimed.
6. Native gameplay and the Mac checks remain open, so the game is not complete.

Verification: `compose_image.py build` and `stages` (every stage hash matches, structured 0), `data_asm.py audit` and `check` for 001 to 005, `tests.test_compose_image` (83, 3 changed groups of expectations, 1 new), `tests.test_data_asm` (36, 5 new), `decomp-progress/test_progress` AuditedBatchTests (19, 1 new; the whole file ends with the same 3 failures and 3 errors in playable-status/ledger setup as on main), `test_function_ledger` (239), `test_function_xrefs` (68), `test_audit_tool_assisted` (53), `test_gen_fragments` (9), `test_verify_matches` (13), `extract_function_evidence.py --check` and `build_function_ledger.py --check` (585 ported, unchanged), `report_image_coverage.py --publish`. Full suite not run: only Track M scripts, their tests, docs and pinned evidence hashes changed.

## Handoff: Track M data slice 3, headers, import tables, resource tree and padding rebuilt (2026-10-20 PDT, cloud thread)

Result: the last **23,430 data bytes** (PE headers 4,096, zero fill after `.text` 3,494, import address table 1,356, import directory with hint/name entries and `.rdata` zero fill 9,968, resource directory tree 2,080, zero fill between resource payloads 2,436) now reassemble byte for byte with the pinned `i686-w64-mingw32-as` 2.43.1 and are bound by the composer. The map counts **data 298,406 of 298,406** (was 274,976); code is unchanged at 1,201,488. Same class `tool_assisted_reconstructed_data`; no whole-file x86 match is claimed.

What changed:
- `scripts/data_asm.py`: manifest profile `structures` (header says `# pointers: not detected (PE structures)`, its own "not claimed" lines), `gen --structures pe --batch 003` and `gen --structures resource --batch 004`, `structure_ranges()` (the complement of the payload batches), the file headers as the pseudo-section `<headers>` (`containing_section`). Batches 001 and 002 regenerate byte-identically (their manifests, layouts and hashes are unchanged).
- `asm_data/data-003` (4 ranges, 18,914 bytes, 680 labels) and `data-004` (23 ranges, 4,516 bytes, 23 labels): manifest, report, payload-free layout. They are extract-at-build like 001/002 (the full source is a build output under `work/asm_data/`, never committed). Two batches because `.rsrc` has a different VA bias (file offset 0x16a000, RVA 0x23f000) from the rest. No pointers are detected: an import-table or IAT entry is an RVA, not a VA, and a Rich-header word can look like an address by chance.
- `compose_image.py`: bound `structures` ranges replace the structured items inside them. The structured groups (`dos_header`, `rich_header`, `pe_headers`, `section_table`, `header_padding`, `iat`, `import_directory`, `resource_tree`, `section_padding`) are still generated from `image/*.json` and compared with the original first (`structured_mismatches`); an item wholly inside a bound range is then dropped, one straddling an edge is an error (`supersede_structured`), `structured_replaced_by_data_batches` in the coverage report counts them, and `compose_image.py build` now also exits 1 when a structured description disagrees with the original. Only jump tables and `int 3` padding (8,070 bytes) stay `structured`.
- `decomp-progress/generate.py`: data ranges may be in `<headers>` and in the zero fill past `.text`'s virtual size (`.text` code range still refused).
- `AGENTS.md` ruling (6) wording: the PE headers, import tables, resource tree and padding are now batches 003 and 004. **For Glib to know:** the 2026-10-19 card named initialized data and resource payloads; I applied the same method to these groups on the coordinator's brief and said so in `AGENTS.md`; there is no separate owner ruling for them. Nothing else about publishing changed: no layout carries a value, no full source is committed.

The 11,530 code bytes the map does not count as matched code (the coordinator asked why): the map's code total 1,213,018 is the whole `.text` virtual size, split into function spans, and a span also holds the function's data items. Of those 11,530 bytes, 5,856 are switch jump tables, 2,214 `int 3` padding, 1,934 index tables and 1,526 alignment padding. 11,290 of them are data items of audited M03 functions (reassembled byte for byte in the batch audits, composer-bound as structured, index-table and padding classes) that baseline mode excludes from matched code by design; 240 are the tables of the two CRT block copies, which only the composer's structured group generates (no assembler audit). So the map's code bar stays at 1,201,488 of 1,213,018 (99.05 percent) until a ledger or a span split moves those items out of the code atoms; I did not change that accounting (it would move 11,290 bytes from code to data and change both totals).

Not claimed: any value, name or field is meaningful, original source or compiler provenance, the uninitialized tail of `.data`, or a whole-file x86 match. The composer hash equality is still composer evidence only.

Verification: `data_asm.py audit` and `check` for 001 to 004 (all accepted, 18,914 and 4,516 bytes identical, 0 relocations), `compose_image.py build` (hash equals the original; structured 8,070, reconstructed 1,503,354, referenced 0), `report_image_coverage.py --publish`, `tests.test_data_asm` (27, 3 new), `tests.test_compose_image` (79, 3 new, mutant tests adjusted: a mutated structured description is still reported and fails the CLI but no longer reaches the composed hash), `extract_function_evidence.py --write` and `build_function_ledger.py --write` (585 ported, unchanged; only pinned test hashes). 
Review: one Sonnet `slice-reviewer` pass on the diff, no P1. Fixed: (P2) `compose_image.py stages` ignored structured mismatches now that 9 groups no longer reach the hash (a stage also needs its descriptions to agree; those 9 groups are replaced at every stage, so the stages run really exercises `jump_tables` and `int3_padding`); (P2) a structured item lost from a description would have been filled silently by the batch, so the replaced items must now tile every bound structures range (`supersede_structured`, adjacent ranges count as one span); (P3) unknown manifest profile refused by the auditor and composer, a structures manifest must claim exactly the `structure_ranges()` derived ranges, `gen --structures` needs an explicit `--batch` and refuses `--range`/`--resources`, tests for each, a map test for a `.text` range straddling the virtual end. Not changed: the `AGENTS.md` ruling (6) wording stays as above and **needs Glib's confirmation** (reviewer P2: it edits an owner ruling on the coordinator's brief; if Glib objects, revert that clause and the map's 298,406 count falls back to 274,976 by dropping batches 003 and 004 from `image/reconstructed.json`); no `<headers>` out-of-range map test (the map fixture has no headers section); `.long [n]` masks the byte length only of every header field and import RVA, which is the existing layout design.

Full suite: not run for this slice. The change touches only Track M scripts (`data_asm.py`, `compose_image.py`, `decomp-progress/generate.py`), their tests, docs and pinned hashes; no runtime, action-family or native source changed. Instead: `tests.test_data_asm` (31), `tests.test_compose_image` (81), `tests.test_function_ledger` (239), `tests.test_function_xrefs` (68), `tests.test_audit_tool_assisted` (53), `tests.test_gen_fragments` (9), `tests.test_verify_matches` (13) pass; `test_build_freshness` (2 failures, 1 error) and `test_extract_function_evidence` (76 errors: missing private receipts) fail identically on clean main 237bc7d; `decomp-progress/test_progress`: 3 failures and 3 errors in the playable-status tests (the known stale playable status; this run did not re-run them on main), every data test passes, the 2 new ones included. `extract_function_evidence.py --check`, `build_function_ledger.py --check` and `compose_image.py stages` pass.

## Handoff: Track M data slice 2, data payloads extracted at build time (2026-10-19 PDT, cloud thread)

What changed: the two full data sources of PR 144 (`asm_data/data-001.s`, `data-002.s`: the game's own strings, numbers and resource bytes as directive text) are no longer committed. `scripts/data_asm.py` regenerates them from the owner's local `GAME.EXE` into `work/asm_data/` (git-ignored) when it generates, audits or checks a batch, and `compose_image.bind_data_batches` regenerates them in memory at build time. The repository keeps the generator, the manifest `asm_data/data-NNN.json` (ranges, range hashes, SHA-256 of the full source and of the layout), the audit report, and a layout `asm_data/data-NNN.layout`: the source with every string, numeric dword and byte list masked as `[length]`. Labels, offsets, zero runs, pointer expressions and the named `.set` address constants of pointer targets stay (structure: which dwords are pointers and where they point); no string, number or byte value does.

Counting is unchanged: 274,976 data bytes still reassemble byte for byte with the pinned `i686-w64-mingw32-as` 2.43.1, the composer still binds them as `tool_assisted_reconstructed_data`, and the composed hash still equals the original. A source counts only when its SHA-256 equals the manifest's and the report's and its masked form equals the committed layout; the layout audit is an allowlist of the line shapes the generator writes (`data_asm.audit_layout`), also enforced by the map generator, which runs without the original. Manifest names must be plain `data-NNN.s` / `data-NNN.layout`.

Wording: `AGENTS.md` ruling (6), `asm/README.md`, `image/README.md`, `WORK-SLICES.md` and `decomp-progress/README.md` now describe the extract-at-build form. It was implemented as the recommended default of the coordinator's card; the owner has not recorded a different choice.

Not changed, for Glib to know: the full sources remain in git history (PR 144); only a history rewrite would remove them. Pointer targets (code, data and import addresses) remain visible in the layouts.

Review: one Sonnet `slice-reviewer` pass on the diff. P1 (a variable in `audited_data_evidence` shadowed the layout dict; the map generator crashed on the real repo) fixed, and the map was regenerated from the real tree. P2 fixed: layout allowlist replaces the two-shape denylist. P2 reworded: pointers are structure, not a value, in all docs. P3 fixed: plain-name check on manifest paths, missing layout reported as a clean error, stale doc lines.

Verification: `tests.test_data_asm` (24), `tests.test_compose_image` (76 before the review fixes; rerun in the full suite), `decomp-progress/test_progress` data tests, `data_asm.py audit` and `check` on both batches, `extract_function_evidence.py --write` and `build_function_ledger.py --write` (557 ported, unchanged; only the pinned test-file hashes changed).

## Handoff: Track M data slice 1, .rdata, .data and resource payloads bound (2026-10-19 PDT, cloud thread)

Glib's ruling (decision card, 10:57 AM PDT 2026-10-19): data and resources rebuilt as sources with machine-named labels count as matched when they reassemble byte for byte. Recorded as ruling (6) in `AGENTS.md` (which also now permits the `asm_data/` sources in the public repo) and in `asm/README.md`.

Result: **274,976 data bytes reassemble byte for byte with the pinned `i686-w64-mingw32-as` 2.43.1** and are bound in the composer; 0 bytes are referenced any more, and the composed image hash equals the original (composer evidence only, not an x86 match claim). New class `tool_assisted_reconstructed_data`, reported apart from code, functions, index tables and padding.
- `asm_data/data-001.s` (+ manifest and report; since the extract-at-build slice a build output, not committed): `.rdata` 0x12a54c to 0x134910 and `.data` 0x137000 to 0x16a000, 250,820 bytes, 10,181 labels (32,352 lines).
- `asm_data/data-002.s` (build output): the 22 resource payload runs from `image/resources.json`, 24,156 bytes, 454 labels (2,672 lines; no pointer detection).
- `scripts/data_asm.py` (`gen`, `audit`, `check`): classifies by heuristic only (NUL-ended ASCII of 4+ printable bytes is `.asciz` / `STR_<va>`, UTF-16LE of 4+ characters is `.string16` / `WSTR_<va>`, aligned zero runs `.fill`, aligned dwords that point into a mapped section are pointers, the rest `.long` / `.byte`; labels `DAT_<va>`). Pointers into the source are `LABEL - IMG + origin`, so a misplaced label changes the bytes; pointers outside it are `.set` constants named `FUN_/CODE_/BSS_/IAT_/RSRC_/OUT_<va>` that must equal their name. The auditor is a lexical whitelist (no `.incbin`, no free byte lists beyond the data directives, fill size capped), pins the assembler by version line plus self-test, requires zero relocations, byte equality over every range and every label at its address; `check` regenerates the sources from the local original and requires them identical.
- `compose_image.py`: `data_batches` in `image/reconstructed.json`; `bind_data_batches` re-checks the report, manifest hash, source hash, pinned assembler, relocations, lexical audit and generator regeneration before it takes the assembled bytes. `image/ranges.json` is now empty. `decomp-progress/generate.py` counts audited data batches as matched data (274,976 of 298,406) with the same checks.

Not claimed: any name or comment is meaningful, original source or compiler provenance for any data, an x86 match of the whole file, or that the remaining 23,430 data-section bytes (PE headers, import directory and IAT, resource directory tree, padding) are rebuilt as data sources; the map does not count them as matched data. The strings, tables and resource payloads now sit in the public repo as directive text (owner ruling (6)); that is a visibility change Glib should confirm is acceptable.

Review: one Sonnet `slice-reviewer` pass, no P1. Fixed: the composer now re-runs the lexical audit and the generator regeneration check instead of trusting the report; the map evidence reader checks batch identity, hashes, section bounds and overlap; a comment containing a quote no longer fools the lexical audit; composer and map negative tests added (edited source, stale report, rehashed `.incbin`, non-generator source, bound plus referenced).

Switch tables and `int 3` padding stay with the structured groups and are skipped; a switch table nothing structured generates, or padding that only partly overlaps a structured range, is refused. These classes are never counted as functions or as code bytes (the code total fell from 1,203,014 to 1,201,550 because the 1,464 padding bytes were typed code before). `ranges.json` was re-derived (all other descriptions identical).

Classification choices: nop-equivalent alignment padding is its own class, not a function; index tables are data items of their owning function. Not claimed: any name or comment is meaningful, original C++ or compiler provenance, or that a derived entry is a function (515 of the 564 have no direct caller; they are code the span split reproduces exactly), or an x86 match of `.data`, `.rdata`, resources or headers.

Left for a Glib rule (22 referenced code ranges, 1,619 bytes): the 12 excluded indexed functions (two unsplit CRT block copies `0x0050ed50`, `0x0050fa90`, the fall-through `0x00513843`, nine noreturn CRT funclets; 1,548 bytes) and 10 derived entries of the same two kinds: four end in a `call` with no data after it (`0x0050e8e2 0x0050f459 0x0050f724 0x0050ff75`, 40 bytes) and six fall through into the next range (`0x00510a4b 0x0051aaf4 0x0051cb35 0x0051dfef 0x0051e24e 0x0051ebf2`, 31 bytes). One rule per kind (a call that never returns; a fall-through fragment bound together with its successor) would let all 22 go in.

Tools: `scripts/annotate_machine.py` gained `--derived`; `audit_tool_assisted.py` and `annotate_machine.py check` now analyze only indexed entries explicitly (derived ones are found from their parent span). `annotate_machine.py check` regenerates all 4,644 machine-annotated sources with 0 differing. The pinned binutils 2.43.1 was rebuilt in the cloud from the Ubuntu orig tarball (SHA-512 equals the pin). Ledger: 231 ported, 128 not needed, 3,829 pending (unchanged; derived entries are not ledger functions), regenerated.

Verification: narrow suites (`test_compose_image` 28 including 5 new data-item tests, `test_audit_tool_assisted` with a batch-009 evidence test, `test_gen_hinted_asm`, `test_function_ledger`, `test_verify_matches`) pass; `annotate_machine.py check`, `extract_function_evidence.py --check`, `build_function_ledger.py --check` pass. ## Handoff: FL-12 Vorbis parity rule, 178 functions ported (2026-10-16, Pacific)

Ledger: **231 ported, 128 not needed, 3,829 pending** (was 53 / 128 / 4,007). All 178 `vorbis`-region functions are now `ported` as `host_adapter_tested`. Glib's ruling (option A on the decision card, answered 7:43 AM PDT 2026-10-16, relayed by the coordinator session): decoded audio equal in length and within 1 LSB of the original counts as parity. Parity numbers from the PR 81 harness run on the Mac: 2,406 Ogg-bearing HIS files, 25 byte-equal, 2,381 within exactly 1 LSB, 0 beyond, 0 native failures, 0 original failures.

What changed: `function-ledger-overrides.json` gains `vorbis_parity_rule` (id `V-PCM-LSB1`: ruling, criterion, numbers, the harness path and hash, limits) and 178 acceptances. `decomp-progress/function_ledger.py` lifts the Vorbis floor only for a port acceptance of class `host_adapter_tested` while that rule is present and valid (numbers add up, nothing beyond 1 LSB, harness hash still matches); `not_needed` entries and rules still never take Vorbis, and a ledger with a ported Vorbis entry but no rule is rejected. The ledger carries the rule as an optional top-level `vorbis_parity_rule`. `src/his_audio.c` has a comment-only block above `his_audio_decode_memory` citing the 178 starts and 25 derived parts (the ledger needs a native source citation per function); `tests/test_vorbis_parity_rule.py` keeps that block, the recorded numbers and the ledger in step.

Limits (also in the rule): the per-file Mac report is not committed and the cloud cannot rerun the harness (it needs the CDSound files), so the numbers are the Mac session's; the native decoder is FFmpeg's, not a port of libvorbis, so this is output parity and not a per-function port or execution claim; the x87 control word 0x027f is an assumption (two files rerun at 0x037f were identical). If `tests/his_vorbis_decoder_original_oracle.py` changes, the rule fails closed until the Mac reruns it and the hash is updated. Replacing FFmpeg with a real libvorbis port (exact byte equality) would be a separate, optional slice.

Verification: `extract_function_evidence.py --check` and `build_function_ledger.py --check` pass; test_function_ledger (226), test_vorbis_parity_rule (3), test_extract_function_evidence, test_function_xrefs, test_record_function_execution pass (437 together, with `/private/tmp` created). Whole `unittest discover` on the cloud box (666 tests; the Mac-only suites do not import here) gives the identical 155 failing tests as clean `origin/main` (658 tests), so 0 new. `decomp-progress/test_progress.py` still fails on the stale playable-status evidence, as on main. One Sonnet `slice-reviewer` read the diff: no P1. Its P2s were fixed: a vacuous not-needed test now asserts the floor message, validator tests cover the entry-level check, the rule id and the file-count sum, and the rule's limits now say it is bound only to the harness hash (a change to `his_audio.c` decoding, `his_pcm_quantize.c` or FFmpeg needs a Mac rerun) and that the cited tests keep the citation in step but do not decode audio. Left alone: `status.json` still says `original_vorbis_equivalence: "unverified"` and `decomp-progress/REPORT.md` shows Vorbis 0 ported (both regenerate with the Mac `collect --write` refresh). Map: `/mnt/project-files/progress-map-after-vorbis.png`.

## Handoff: FL-9 Vorbis PCM parity ran, NOT accepted (2026-10-16 12:25 AM PDT)

Glib's ruling (2026-10-15 9:59 PM PDT): the 178 statically linked Vorbis functions count as ported only if the original decoder and the native FFmpeg adapter give byte-equal PCM on the real CDSound Ogg files. Result over all 2,406 Ogg-bearing `.HIS` files: first run **20 match, 2,366 mismatch, 20 native decode failures**; after the `his_audio.c` short-stream fix below, **25 match, 2,381 mismatch, 0 native failures** (25 + 2,381 = 2,406; 2,406 of 2,406 original decodes succeeded). So the byte-equality condition is not met, the ledger is unchanged (17 ported, 4,043 pending, Vorbis still pending) and no acceptances, overrides or ledger regeneration were done.

Harness: `tests/his_vorbis_decoder_original_oracle.py` (`python3 outputs/blackmoor-decomp/tests/his_vorbis_decoder_original_oracle.py --sample 24 --jobs 8 --report work/analysis/FL-9/sample24.json`, or no `--sample` for all files; it re-executes itself under `work/tools/oracle-python`). It runs the unmodified original libvorbisfile code from the hash-pinned GAME.EXE under Unicorn the way the game's loader does: `ov_open_callbacks` `0x4fcff0` on a `FILE` positioned after the 30-byte HIS header, then `ov_read` `0x4fe2d0` (word 2/signed for 16-bit, 1/unsigned for 8-bit) until the header's decoded byte count is filled. Host stubs: CRT `malloc 0x50ea90`, `calloc 0x50e97e`, `realloc 0x50ebca`, `free 0x50e936`, `_errno 0x50ea7e`, `__doserrno 0x50ea87` and the four stdio callbacks; everything else (Ogg framing, floor/residue, MDCT, windowing, float to PCM) executes from the image. Native side: `his_audio_decode_memory` (FFmpeg) then `his_pcm_quantize_planar`. The x87 control word is 0x027f (MSVC/Win32 default; an assumption); two files rerun at 0x037f (`Icant`, `NSHT01`) gave identical results. Full run: 1,102 s on 9 processes. Raw reports `work/analysis/FL-9/all.json` (before the fix) and `all-after-fix.json` (per-file counts, byte hashes, differing-sample statistics; not committed, it is derived from game audio).

What differs:
1. **2,366 files differ by exactly one LSB**, never more (max |delta| is 1 in every one of them), on a small share of samples (0.005% to about 0.3% in a 24-file stratified sample, for example 1 of 18,262 on `Pot03`, 952 of 752,408 on `MDC66`). Sample count and length are equal. This is the float-level difference between FFmpeg's native Vorbis decoder and libvorbis, which rounds across a .5 boundary after the 16-bit quantizer. It is not a quantizer defect (the quantizer is already oracle-verified) and not an x87 precision-control effect.
2. **(Fixed in this PR.) 20 files failed in the native adapter outright** (`Aeolus_Pick_up`, `BallClunk01`, `CLICK`, `CONfootsteps02`, `ChestClose01`, `Clank_Metal02`, `Click_MusicBox`, `Click_computer_double`, `Key_Turn_Open`, `KnockWood_Light02`, `Metal_Move06`, `ND_Gasp01`, `PaperFold07`, `Pill_Shake02`, `PuzzleBoxLidOn02`, `Silent_Grenny`, `SpoonSetDown03`, `TilePutDown` and two more in `all.json`). The adapter reports "Vorbis produced N of N+128 declared HIS frames": FFmpeg yields 128 frames fewer than the header declares (`his_audio.c` fails when the stream ends early), while the original fills the declared length. In the native app these files could not load, so this was a real playability defect, separate from the parity ruling.
   Fix (`his_audio.c`, decision taken by the coordinator): the decoder now sets `AV_CODEC_FLAG2_SKIP_MANUAL` and `append_decoded_frame` applies only the leading skip FFmpeg reports (always the first 128-sample frame, which every one of the 2,406 files hits) and never the trailing one, so the samples past the final granule are kept like libvorbisfile does; output is still capped at the declared frame count. A first attempt that dropped the leading skip as well shifted every sample (so the leading skip is required). Test: `test_ogg_streams_shorter_than_the_header_by_the_final_granule_still_decode` (three real files; compares with the FFmpeg CLI prefix and requires exactly 128 extra frames). The first reviewer's check on all files: the 2,386 previously decodable files have identical native hashes, the 20 gain exactly 128 frames, and an ASan/UBSan run over all 2,509 HIS files is clean. Known limits (reviewer P2/P3): the nonzero-offset plane shift is never exercised on real data (the only shipped skip equals the frame length and returns early; arithmetic verified on a synthetic FLTP/FLT/S16/S16P harness, no repo test), a skip larger than the first frame is clamped, not carried (not present in any shipped file), and about 74 files decode longer than their header and are truncated at the header count, as the original's read loop does.
3. 25 files match byte for byte (for example `Icant`, `CLICK`, `Card_Deal01`, `Eth_Footsteps`, `Thunder03`, `VoxDialAgain01`); the other 2,381 are the 1-LSB class in item 1.

Options for Glib (still open, nothing accepted): (a) loosen the ruling to "equal within 1 LSB with identical length", which the 2,381 files satisfy and a gate could encode, but it is a ruling and not a byte match; (b) make the native side exact by porting libvorbis's float path (a native Vorbis decoder, or the original code as a tool-assisted reconstruction), which is the only route to byte equality. Option (c), the short-stream fix, is done.

Verified: the harness ran end to end on all 2,406 files before and after the fix with 0 harness errors; all audio test modules (`test_*audio*.py`, 33 tests, 5 skipped) and `test_his_pcm_quantize.py` pass on the final code; one Sonnet `slice-reviewer` read the diff (no P1, no P2 defect; its P2 coverage gap and P3s are listed above). Full suite (`python3 -m unittest discover`, 2,814 tests, 940 s, run before the last one-line skip clamp): 113 failures and 1 error, none in audio code. They are mostly `test_build_freshness` stale-artifact failures (about 90), oracle and `scene_resource` failures against stale native libraries, and ledger/evidence checks that need the private evidence sources; I could not run the baseline on a clean `origin/main` (no helper worktrees) or the canonical `script/run_test_suite.sh`, so "not caused by this change" is inferred, not measured. Blocker found: `scripts/build_action_family_0c_runtime.sh` was committed with mode 100644 (RT-20), so `script/build_all_native_checks.sh` stops there with "Permission denied" in a fresh checkout; whoever owns RT-20 should `git update-index --chmod=+x` it. Ledger: `extract_function_evidence.py --write` then `build_function_ledger.py --write` only re-pinned the changed `his_audio.c`, `test_his_audio.py` and new oracle hashes (counts unchanged, no acceptance); `--check` passes for both and `test_function_ledger.py` (221) and `test_extract_function_evidence.py` (140) pass after the re-pin. Progress map: not regenerated (nothing moved).

CRT exp `0x50de00` (thunk to the x87 exp at `0x51397b`) was NOT ported: Unicorn 2.1.4's `f2xm1` is a double-precision approximation (measured about 1e-16 relative error against 2^x - 1, real hardware is about 1e-19), so a bit-exact match to the oracle would model an emulator quirk, not the original. `0x44753b` therefore stays pending; it needs a hardware-faithful reference for the weight (a Glib ruling or a native long-double reference).

## Handoff: FL-7 library rules, 128 functions not needed, ported still 12 (2026-10-15 8:50 PM PDT)

Ledger: **12 ported, 128 not_needed, 4,048 pending** (was 12 / 0 / 4,176). The library rules close functions as `not_needed`, never as `ported`, so the ported count did not move. By reason: compiler_eh_funclet 76 (72 by rule R-EH, 4 reviewed Catch@ entries), win32_plumbing_no_observable_effect 25, host_libc_standard_contract 12, crt_startup_termination 10, library_internal_unreached_by_game 5. Region crt_mfc: 1,374 pending, of which 460 are game-parent funclets that follow their parents, 849 library functions the readers would not waive (below), and the 9 Catch@ handlers that swallow or convert exceptions.

How it was done (cloud, from `work-private.tar.gz`, no Mac): two Sonnet readers classified all 929 crt_mfc library functions from Ghidra pseudocode (category plus a reason only when sure; 80 got a reason). The root applied mechanical gates on top (host libc only from a named list of plain str*/mem* routines; startup only with no game caller and not reachable from game code; win32 only with imports inside the allowlist and no indirect calls). A second independent Sonnet reader audited every one of the 57 proposed entries (56 agreed; `0x51ca36` was refused, it sets DC colours). The ledger then refused six more through its own gates (two unsplit census spans, and four functions in the semantics_sensitive closure: `_ioinit`, `_heap_init`, `__sbh_heap_init`, `0x525917`). A third reader read all 59 sampled and then all 73 candidate funclets.

Code changes: (1) `rule_r_eh` now refuses a funclet that dispatches indirectly or jumps into code holding an indirect jump (a virtual-dispatch thunk). The first 59-entry sample found 4 defects of exactly that kind (`0x528e3c`, `0x528fd4`, `0x528ff4`, `0x5290d8`: the funclet is `mov ecx,[ebp-0x10]; jmp` to a vtable thunk, and Ghidra shows an empty body), so the sample was not accepted, the predicate was tightened, and the 73 remaining candidates were read in full. One more defect, `0x528f50` (restores a routing-frame field; the save half is not visible), is excluded through the new R-EH parameter `excluded` (it must be a candidate of the rule, the ledger checks). R-EH is accepted with n = 72 (the clean ones, hash of their addresses) and defects = 0; the caveats in the overrides file say all this. (2) `crt_startup_termination` now also refuses code that game code reaches through direct calls transitively (`game_reached` in `function_ledger.py`). (3) `win32_import_allowlist` (102 imports) is the default ruling below.

Rulings taken by default (Glib can overturn): (a) The allowlist holds only queries, rect and metric helpers, synchronization, TLS, resource lookup, global memory and atoms, and GDI state calls. It leaves out everything that can deliver a window message and so run game code (SendMessage, PostMessage, SetWindowLong, SetWindowPos, EnableWindow, ShowWindow, DestroyWindow, DefWindowProc), input, cursor, text drawing, file, registry, time, heap, process and thread control. (b) R-EH covers library-parent funclets only; game-parent funclets (460) follow once their parent is ported, with a fresh review. (c) 18 covered funclets call a library destructor that makes a virtual call, and 4 destroy a borrowed handle; both are accepted because they run only while a library function unwinds an exception, which the native port has no counterpart for. Refusing the 18 would drop the covered set to 54, under the 59 sample minimum. (d) R-LIB-INTERNAL stays unaccepted (10 candidates, below 59); its five clean functions are per-function entries, the five x87 helpers stay pending. (e) strcmp, strncmp, memcmp: the original returns -1, 0 or 1 and the host only guarantees the sign; callers were not checked for sign-only use (`0x4348b0`, `0x51d862`), so confirm when those callers are ported.

What was not closed, and why: 221 functions the readers marked libc_sensitive (rand, float conversion, formatting, time, file and stdio, malloc and heap, 64-bit helpers, multibyte conversion) stay pending because the ledger says their behaviour must be ported or adapter-tested, never waived; the 513 MFC and CRT internals with game-observable results (containers, strings, archives, handle maps, message dispatch) stay pending; Vorbis (178) waits for FL-9. This cannot move the ported count by hundreds: that needs adapter-tested or ported classes (FL-9 for Vorbis, FL-6 batches for the game).

Verified: `build_function_ledger.py --check`, `extract_function_xrefs.py --check`, the ledger, xrefs and evidence suites. One Sonnet reviewer read the diff (no P1; its P2 on the sample record is answered by the caveats and the `excluded` candidate check, its P2 on indirect calls by ruling (c)). Open reviewer P3s: the startup check ignores address-taken and indirect game callers (all ten entries have only CRT startup parents), `win32` entries may call undecided library callees (for example `_lock` calls the allocator), `0x51c47f` CalcWindowRect computes window size. The full suite on Linux is reported in the PR.

Next: FL-9 Vorbis PCM parity, then FL-6 batches (the four near-miss static symbols named in the FL-8 handoff need a direct test); when parents of game funclets are ported, add `game` to `eh_parent_regions` with a fresh read.

## Handoff: FL-8 executed-range hooks, 12 functions ported (2026-10-15 8:05 PM PDT)

Ledger: **12 ported, 4,176 pending, 0 not needed** (was 5 of 4,188): 8 oracle-verified, 3 tests-only, 1 adapter. New: `0x42ae73` action0b_step, `0x4daf6e` input_consume_field4, `0x4eaadc` action78_visit, `0x4e9e84` action78_add_item, `0x4ea06c` action79_remove_item, `0x4c7314` cif_pixels_convert_555, `0x4e1a7e` action_ui_initialize_controls; `0x42ba3a` moved from tests-only to oracle-verified. Ran in the cloud from the private `work-private.tar.gz` (Unicorn 2.1.4 via pip; a scratch `xcrun` shim turns the Mac build scripts into Linux clang builds, so the native tests ran here on x86_64 Linux, not macOS arm64).

What FL-8 is: `scripts/exec_trace/sitecustomize.py` adds one Unicorn code hook to every oracle (installed before each `emu_start`, so it runs after the oracle's own hooks; an instruction an oracle replaced by a Python seam is not counted; wall-clock timeouts are scaled x20 while recording). `scripts/record_function_execution.py run --root COPY --trace-dir DIR` runs every `tests/*oracle*.py` (arguments for the six that need them are in `ORACLE_ARGS`), `collect --write` merges the runs that exited 0 into the committed `function-execution.json` (addresses, hit counts, script hashes, no bytes; only bytes inside the function's code size count). Run the oracles from a scratch copy of the checkout so they do not rewrite the private slice receipts. 95 of 109 scripts recorded; the 14 that did not need the game data (avf, caption), a Mac toolchain (live audio, startup), the pinned GNU as (matching_s08_*; excluded on purpose, they are Track M) or fail on data (condition_extra census, dependency case 737 fixed by the timeout scale, 3e/3f loader). The extractor turns each (function, script) pair into an oracle pointer: `use_basis: executed` when the function was entered and every code byte ran, otherwise `executed_partial` (mapped, never qualifies). The ledger now requires an `executed` pointer for `native_oracle_verified`; declared window starts and hook lists no longer qualify, and a partly executed window is not a full window for `candidate_port`. This also settles the "oracle window without a test path" question: an executed pointer always names the script.

Review: 30 candidate pairs went to three Sonnet readers (native body against Ghidra pseudocode, the test, the oracle). 11 accepted, 19 rejected. Rejected for the symbol: 0x48945b, 0x4faf98, 0x4f709c, 0x4c0774, 0x4c08d3, 0x4d36ed, 0x50ce04, 0x4fc05d, 0x4e7ba7, 0x4e7bf3, 0x4e8dc8, 0x4ee2a9, 0x4ee5cd, 0x43ec4a/85/c0/ed71 (one dispatcher serves four originals; `session_input_feed`), 0x4e444d, 0x4f55d5 (close: oracle never drives the 0x16e/0x172 gates). Real matches that the lexical test rule left out because their symbols are static or no test body names them: `0x4e8d96` put_back, `0x4e3ade` sort_phone_records, `0x447418` controller_setup, `0x4474cf` controller_capture_destination (reader verdict accept, oracle at parent granularity); add a direct test or an exported wrapper and they can be accepted. One independent `slice-reviewer` read the diff (no P1); its P2s on counting thunk bytes and re-installing the hook were fixed; open P2s: "executed" does not prove the oracle compared that function with the native symbol (the reviewer reads the oracle), an oracle that patches original code in memory is still credited, and the 0x4eaadc acceptance names a null-argument test (behaviour evidence is the oracle). Seven direct tests were added (action78_add_item, action79_remove_item, cif_pixels_convert_555, action_ui_initialize_controls) and five native symbols got a leading comment citing the original address.

Full suite (Linux, shim): 2,610 tests, 46 failures/errors. Treat these 46 as **unverified, not as regressions**: none is in code touched here, and they could not be checked off the Mac. They are Mac or toolchain bound (asan runtime missing, no AppKit/ffmpeg, build-freshness against the Mac build dir, oracle-python absent) plus `test_function_xrefs` line 791, which still asserts the FL-3 draft overrides have no acceptances and has failed on main since PR 70. Mac checks still worth a run: the full suite and `script/run_test_suite.sh`.

Next: FL-7 library rules (CRT/MFC, exception funclets) and FL-9 Vorbis PCM parity move the count by hundreds; both need Glib's sampling review, not more oracle time. Rule taken by default: no new Glib rulings were needed.

## Handoff: FL-6 second batch, 5 functions ported (2026-10-15 7:15 PM PDT)

Ledger: **5 ported, 4,183 pending, 0 not needed** (was 0 of 4,188). Ported, all by reviewed acceptance, none oracle-class: `0x40b2d0` action17_cursor_index_for_type, `0x42ba3a` action_core_finish_action, `0x4f6059` sound_control_play, `0x4f6334` session_conditions_query_channel_record (these four tests-only), `0x50cd50` session_conditions_crt_rand (host_adapter_tested). Each native symbol was read against the Ghidra pseudocode, its named tests pass on macOS arm64 (test_session_bindings_conditions ran 35 with 1 skip), and the acceptance `note` in `function-ledger-overrides.json` lists known gaps. The notes live in that file only; the ledger entries and the panel do not carry them. One Sonnet `slice-reviewer` (accept-after-fixes, no P1) reviewed the diff; its P2s on the tests were fixed (added the `raw_control != 0` Play case and the success-path state asserts; the 0x4f6334 acceptance now also cites the two opcode-17 leaf tests). `0x44753b` action_family28_run_sequence was dropped from the batch: its oracle lets curve-weight mismatches against the original pass (`weight_tolerance_ulp: 0`, cause unresolved), so "ported" would overclaim; it is a good candidate once that is settled.

Known limits of the new tooling (from the review, unchanged): a test pointer is lexical, and a test docstring that names the symbol counts as a pointer (an author's claim a reviewer must read, not proof; the ledger's own check re-reads the test text but does not follow helper calls); unlocated unverified items never block, and a located item binds only to the function containing its address, so an unverified statement about a callee does not block its caller; the extractor only reads receipt keys matching `UNVERIFIED_KEY` and the safety-rejection spellings.

What changed in the tooling: (1) `extract_function_evidence.py` now names the test case: every `test*` function of a `tests/test_*.py` file, or any function of a `tests/*.c|mm` harness, that mentions the symbol of one of a function's native_source pointers gets a `test` pointer with its `test_id` (identifiers in the body, bare-identifier strings, and the test's docstring; comments do not count); `--work PATH` points it at the private tree, which a git worktree does not have. (2) The ledger accepts `blockers_reviewed: [{id, rationale}]` in an acceptance and carries `unlocated_unverified` on receipt pointers. (3) `scripts/audit_port_candidates.py` lists which `candidate_port` functions pass the mechanical checks (read-only; `--va`, `--symbol`, `--json`).

**Ruling taken by default, for Glib to overturn (blockers):** a receipt blocker that is not a recorded safety rejection clears only through `blockers_reviewed` in the acceptance: the id must be one of that function's blockers, the rationale (at least 40 characters) says why the unverified branch does not touch what the acceptance claims, and the ledger entry keeps the blocker, now carrying `{reviewer, date, rationale}`. A review of a blocker that is already a safety rejection is an error, and a pending entry cannot carry one. Unlocated unverified items (no address) are shown as a count on the receipt pointer and never block. To overturn: delete `blockers_reviewed` from `load_overrides` and `check_acceptance` in `decomp-progress/function_ledger.py`. Used once: `0x4f6334` (RT-14 item about overlays without a sound provider).

Why only 6 of 151: three read-only audits of 33 proposed pairs found the automated proposal wrong or partial for most. The proposer takes any native symbol whose body or comment contains the address, which often names a callee or a branch. Rejected, with the right target where known: `0x410f99` (left, as instructed), `0x44753b` (see above), `0x41c3ba` (a callee of the 0x41c7f4 constructor; only a byte compare in the A-3E oracle covers it), `0x414ceb` avf_open (oracle compares it, but the named tests do not call it and native rejects inputs the original accepts: bpp other than 16, short tables), `0x4c0774` (a branch of action35_runtime_step), `0x4c08d3` (really the static `rect()` in action_family_35_runtime.c), `0x4c0ab1` (static `triggers()`, indirect oracle, no test names it), `0x4ccef1` (really avf_player_stop, which serves four originals), `0x4dc182`, `0x4f52a9`/`0x4f61e7`/`0x4f7207` (live_audio adapter pieces, not 1:1), `0x4bef85`, `0x4bf10f`, `0x4d36ed`, `0x4dad60`, `0x4e9c2f`, `0x4e444d`, `0x4e9add` (really action_family_79_construct), `0x4fc05d`, `0x4cc70f`, `0x43dbd5`, `0x423259` and `0x423da2` (callees). Near misses worth a later look: `0x4f55d5` sound_control_update (1:1, S04-1 oracle compares it, but two blockers need a review and gates 16e/172 are only natively tested), `0x4e9add` via action_family_79_construct, `0x42ba3a` upgrade to oracle class via tests/action_core_original_oracle.py.

Not done, and why: the oracles and original-x86 tests were NOT rerun. They need `work/original/GAME.EXE`, which only the main checkout has (`test_live_audio_restart_oracle.py` etc. skip in a worktree), and running from the main checkout is not an allowed command form here. The classes above are therefore tests-only; where an oracle compares the symbol (0x42ba3a, 0x4f6059) its recorded run exists in `work/analysis/slices` but was not repeated. The blocked build script: `scripts/build_live_audio_restart.sh` is committed non-executable (mode 100644), so it runs only through `sh`/the test that calls it. Mac tests run and passing: test_session_bindings_conditions (35), test_action_family_3e (17), test_action_core (15), test_avf_player (19), test_avf_decoder (30), test_sound_control (10), test_action_family_17_runtime (38), test_action_family_28_sequence (13), test_live_audio_restart (3). The full `unittest discover` suite was run once here and is not meaningful in a worktree: hundreds of tests error because `work/` (private analysis files, the boot chunk, slice receipt directories) is absent; the ledger, extractor and progress-map tests pass (213, 135 and 94). Run the full suite from a checkout that has `work/`.

## Handoff: FL-6 first batch, no function accepted (2026-10-15 5:45 PM PDT)

Result: the ledger is unchanged (0 ported, 4,188 pending). A cloud session without `work/`, the owner's original and the Mac libraries tried to accept a first batch from the 151 `candidate_port` functions and could not, because the ledger's own gates stopped every one. Nothing was marked ported without the evidence the rules require.

What the gates showed (all computed from the committed `function-ledger.json`, `function-evidence.json` and the sources; `build_function_ledger.py --write` runs without the original):
- 66 of 151 candidates carry an uncleared receipt blocker and no way exists to clear one yet (needs the `blockers_reviewed` field named in the FL handoff). 85 have none.
- Of those 85: 74 name a native symbol, 46 have a qualifying oracle window with a path, but only 19 have a test pointer with a `test_id`. An acceptance can only name a test the evidence already lists with an id.
- Trying the eligible combinations: `0x415db9` (`avf_lzss_expand`) is rejected because its only test pointer is `lzss_reference`, a helper, not a test; `0x416af3` (`decode_commands`) because its test never names the symbol or module `avf_decoder`; `0x4e8d96` (`put_back`) because neither listed test names `put_back` or `action_family_78_runtime`, and its oracle window has no test path so it cannot be cited at all (only `native_tests_only` would be open); `0x42b2e4` because the pointer is the header prototype `action0c_step` and its window is `mapped`; `0x461903` because `known_body_length` only holds the 855-byte constant of the A-D3 decoder, not the function.
- Mechanically eligible pairs exist for five functions: `0x4f52a9`, `0x4f6059`, `0x4f7207` (live audio pump, Play wrapper, delayed repeat), `0x4c0774` (Bink finish) and `0x410f99`. Their tests (`tests/live_audio_restart_test.c`, `tests/session_conditions_audio_fixture.c`, `tests/native_a35_glue_test.mm`) build only on macOS arm64 against the Mac audio libraries, and the symbol-to-function match is partial (`play_channel` delegates to the recovered kernel `sound_control_play`; `run_repeat_pass` is one channel of the 0x4f7207 loop; `defaults` for 0x410f99 is not credible). They need a Mac run of those tests and a reviewer who reads the code against the Ghidra pseudocode before any acceptance.

What would unblock FL-6 (all need the Mac, one standalone command each, no new code in the game): (1) improve `extract_function_evidence.py` so a test pointer carries the test that actually exercises the symbol (most tests have `test_id: null`), then `--write` it; (2) let the ledger name an oracle window recorded without a test path, or record the path; (3) add `blockers_reviewed`. Items 2 and 3 change acceptance rules and need Glib's ruling. No acceptance rule was touched here.

## Handoff: test suite pruning (2026-10-15 5:10 PM PDT)

Glib asked for the smallest defensible suite. Removed 21 tests only where they were provably redundant: the 19 `test_constants` tests in `test_action_family_{1c,20,2d,6d,70,76,77,80,9a,aa,d0,d3,d6,da,e0,e4,eb,ee,f5}.py` (they compared test-module constants with literals and never called the native library; each file's decode/construct tests still pin BODY against production), `PortAcceptanceTests.test_fixture_acceptances_are_ported` (identical body to `test_blocker_with_a_matching_safety_divergence_is_allowed`), `LiveAudioTests.test_end_to_end_reports_exact_counts` (`end_to_end_nsht01` is in `EXPECTED_TESTS`, so `test_fixtures_against_real_and_substitute_hosts` asserts the same PASS), plus one repeated assertion in `test_native_app.py` and two unused names. Everything else was kept: three read-only Sonnet surveys of 26 files found no other pair with the same behaviour, conditions and outcome (same-shaped tests across action families exercise different native libraries). Evidence, ledger and progress map were regenerated because the ledger cites those test files.

Baseline (before): 2,611 tests, 2,122 s under load, 17 failures and 3 errors, all caused by stale prebuilt libraries copied into the worktree. After (merged tree with RT-22): 2,745 tests, 1,575 s, the only two failures (native-app glue tests) were duplicate-symbol errors from stray `* 2.o` objects in the copied build directory, and they pass once those are deleted; the ledger and progress tests pass after regeneration. The 2,745 includes the new inventory tests from main, so the raw counts are not like-for-like; the pruned tests ran in well under a second, so the runtime saving from removals is negligible.

Where the time goes (candidates for a decision, none removed): `test_rt20_every_s2010_hotspot_registers_hovers_and_changes_scene` 244 s, `inventory_view_runtime_oracle` 106 s, `action_family_78_runtime_oracle` 62 s, `test_live_audio_restart` and `test_live_audio` mutation sweeps about 35 to 60 s each, and three `test_native_app` tests that run the identical `--live-ticks 3000` command with disjoint assertions (could be merged to save about 80 s, which Glib's brief forbids as a giant combined test). These are unique oracle, mutation or end-to-end coverage, so they need a decision from Glib, not deletion.

Flagged and kept: `test_scene_executor.py::test_public_runtime_and_handler_result_abi_layout_is_unchanged` (ctypes layout pin, calls no production code), `test_tail_oracle.py::test_extended_family_and_length_coverage` (guards the oracle sweep corpus), `test_engine_boot.py::test_malformed_short_descriptor_and_plgo_are_bounds_errors` (weak `assertNotEqual`), the `28` / `28_sequence` frame-count pair, `compose_image` mutant pairs that hit `compose()` versus `referenced_items()`. Unused enum-mirror constants (`ERR_MEMORY`, `PROVIDER_NONE`, `FAIL_NONE` and similar) were left because removing members of enum-shaped assignments is churn. Process note: `script/build_all_native_checks.sh` stops at `build_action_family_0c_runtime.sh` because that file is committed non-executable (mode 100644); run the builders with `sh` instead.

## Handoff: RT-11 sound persistence and RT-11.1 channel restart (2026-10-15 PDT)

Merged in one PR (Codex built RT-11, which never got its review; Codex is out of quota until Oct 19, so root finished it): a scene load no longer unloads every sound. Recovered rule (0x438173 selects state 2 only for a zero transition word 0x5c39ff; 0x4381b5 -> 0x4fb018): a fresh scene entry unloads the MSND channel (BOOT MSND +0x21, global 0x5b451f) and channels 0..13, one tick after the load, nothing else, no fade; a continuation touches no sound. `live_audio_unload_all` is now used only by tests. One Sonnet reviewer over RT-11 (no P1): fixed the stale RT-4 comment, the silent reset-plan rejection (now logs `scene_audio_error detail=reset-plan-rejected`), and added a test that the S5906->S4700 and S4700->S4710 loads carry every loaded channel unchanged.
**RT-11 exposed a gap, RT-11.1 fixes it.** With sounds persisting, S6400's ClockTicking_Grandfather01 (channel 4, raw control 0, stream class) was still loaded in S6401; our mixer let it complete and the opcode-17 adapter then refused ("restart not modelled"), freezing the tutorial at S6401 row 0 (18 `test_native_app` tutorial tests failed). In the original that control-0 stream never completes: the ring filler rewinds at EOF (0x4f5a5e..0x4f5a99), the update 0x4f55d5 never runs for it, and GetStatus keeps reading "playing". `live_audio_service(audio, tick_ms, stats)` (called once per app update) now models the pump 0x4f52a9 / update 0x4f55d5 / delayed repeat 0x4f7207 / Play wrapper 0x4f6059: stream-class control-0 channels play looped on the host, whole-buffer control-0 channels are restarted when they complete, control-nonzero channels never restart. Original-x86 oracle (Unicorn): 164 scenarios (+1 after review), 1,906 steps, 0 mismatches; 13 provider/adapter mutants detected by the C tests; the route `--live-scene-row 6400:17 --live-scene-row 6401:6` ends `status=0 provider=none` instead of freezing at tick 109.
Unverified: flag 0x20 fade stages (refused, not guessed) and flag states 0x3/0x7/0x100/0x300/0x500; flag 0x10 random repeat is implemented and oracle-compared but unreachable from 0x9f scene data (every runtime-0x9f sound has flags 0, repeat range 500..2000); runtime types 0x98 and 0x99 load sounds with nonzero flags (0x42995d writes 0x7, 0x42bd85 writes 0x1) and native has no producer for them, and `ActionSoundAudioCommand` carries no flags field (a future producer must add one); the stream class compare (0x4f3b49, header dword at +24 > 0x56220) is static evidence; control-nonzero streams play fully while the original stops 0x1388 bytes early; stale stop notifications after Stop/Play/unload are dropped natively while the original leaves them queued (three oracle-shaped scenarios diverge: a no-op for an already-playing buffer, but a loaded-not-yet-played sound would be started after unload+load+play); the pump runs on the app tick clock, not GetTickCount; the dialogue-object-owned sound cleanup (0x43df47 -> 0x4730a6 -> 0x4e1d34, object+0xf6) and scene-header sound start 0x4fc341 are unbound (no native producer; the old blanket unload covered it implicitly), as are the other 0x4fb018 call sites (0x42908e, 0x429129, 0x429220, 0x43ab5f, 0x4d4312), fade-stop 0x4fafc7/0x4fb086 and indirect/vtable teardown; the oracle executes the selector and 0x4fb018 reset, not the app-glue timing or the MSND offset/guard (two glue mutants, ignoring `msnd[0x22]` and weakening `length < 0x23`, survive and are unreachable with the real BOOT, channel 27). Everything is headless and offline: no listening pass has been done.

The oracle needs `work/original/GAME.EXE` and is run by hand from `tests/`; the result JSON is private (`work/analysis/slices/RT-24.oracle-result.json`).

**Not verified, honest limits.** The app glue in `blackmoor_app.mm` was syntax-checked against a mock only, not compiled or run (no macOS toolchain in the cloud). Where the render callback draws (Z-order slot, relation to the scene frame) and the saved-frame source `[0x5d15e3]` are stand-ins: the glue uses a back-buffer snapshot. The pointer is fed from the guarded `session_input_tick` projection (modes 2-5, producer disabled and sprite/viewport overlap handled there, per the Codex review). The meaning of `[0x5bb1ca]` is inferred (set to 1 on phase 0). Known native safety differences (oracle D2 to D5, tagged and expected): the native kernel validates the body and radius 0, bounds-checks the blits, owns and frees same-mode buffers, and stops on a host failure where the original has no recovery. The CIFTREE image load result is a host callback. Review notes (one Sonnet diff review): fixed the clamp globals, 0x5831ae/0x5831b2 are the right and bottom of the THIRD VIEW rectangle (`view_words[10]`/`[11]`), not the scene source rectangle (the oracle host supplies them directly, so it could not catch the glue). Left as known limits: the render stand-in snapshots the back buffer lazily, so overlay or caption pixels inside the puzzle window can be overwritten by the stale snapshot (the original restores from the dedicated saved frame `[0x5d15e3]`); a pointer beyond the third rectangle's right edge by more than the radius gives an inverted window, which the glue refuses to draw (`D9_ERR_BOUNDS`, one log line) where the original does not check; `session_ad9_invalidate` is not called from the app (harmless, the render draws every tick); `store_world` is unset so `[0x5bb1ca]` is not mirrored into app state; the glue's slot allocator is a private table, not the shared generic Z table of 0x4d39f9; radius 1 gives a NaN alpha (not in the corpus).

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud); the new glue functions were only syntax-checked on a mock. (2) The save routine `0x4d7a15` is NOT ported and has in-memory effects the stub drops (it zeroes 0xa6c4 dwords at `obj+0x98b0` of the game object `0x5e2e70` and copies a string to `obj+0x333c2` before opening the file), so the 6 scenes where 0x65 was the only missing type are counted as handled at the type level but their save does nothing: the app's save callback logs `scene_a65_save_requested ... persisted=0` and writes nothing, so a SAVE_CONTINUE row completes but the Continue entry has no file to load. (3) One native safety difference: a 0x68/0x69 body byte of 10 or more indexes past the port's ten records; the original clears that record anyway, the native binding refuses it UNSUPPORTED before any effect; the corpus uses 1..8. (4) Readers of the timer table (condition leaves `0x4246bb`, the clock, the task list) are other slices; the oracle never runs them.

Next: raw 0x45 (9 scenes, 32 records; the same table, seven sub-cases, `0x428849` jump table) reuses `session_timer_record_clear` and the `time_record` host callback; then 0x4b (5), 0x1b (4), 0x7d (4) by `scene-action-coverage.json`; porting the save routine `0x4d7a15`; raw 0x2d is RT-36.

## Handoff: RT-38 raw 0x45 timer control runtime (2026-10-16 PDT, cloud thread)

Merged in this slice: `src/session_bindings_a45.[ch]` (one binding, executor handler `session_a45_handler`: DIRECT_COMPLETED with the phase unchanged when a sub-case applies, PENDING with the phase unchanged when it does not), registration `bindings[34]` in `blackmoor_app.mm` (count 35; RT-36 (raw 0x2d) holds 33) and `build_native_app.sh`, `scripts/build_action_family_45_runtime.sh`, packet `tasks/RT-38-timer-control-45-runtime.md`. Dispatcher arm `0x4287cc` of `0x427a50` (gate mode 0): the signed word `body[0]` picks timer record n of the table at `0x585d65` (the same one raw 0x68/0x69 write, RT-37) and the signed word `body[2]` picks one of seven sub-cases through the jump table `0x42ac91`: 0 clears the record with `0x433bd2`; 1 and 2 store the state; 3 and 4 add and subtract hours/minutes/seconds (state 1, 2, 5 or 6 only, subtraction clamps each field at zero, a seconds word of 5000 or more reads the loader word table `0x5adb81`, elapsed ms recomputed); 5 and 6 configure the record (state 1 only: limit values, 49 + 33 + 100 body bytes, the ten event pair index words, the entries, state := sub-case). Any other sub-case, or a state the sub-case does not accept, changes nothing and leaves the row open, so it is visited again next tick. The real corpus uses only sub-cases 0 (4 rows), 5 (6) and 6 (22) on 9 scenes; 3 rows carry a seconds word of 5000, 5001 or 5004, which the app sources from BSUM like the condition leaves.

Scene counts (`scene-action-coverage.json`): scenes with every action type handled (type-level upper bound) 2,013 to 2,019 of 2,078 (+6: 0x45 appears in 9 scenes, 3 of them also need other types). Ported-function count unchanged (231). 0x4b, 0x1b and 0x7d were not folded in: each calls an object method (`0x448d11`, `0x414947`, `0x4eb5f2`) and is its own slice.

Verification: original-x86 oracle `tests/action_family_45_runtime_original_oracle.py` runs the real dispatcher `0x427a50` (the original `0x433bd2`, gate and loader as x86, nothing replaced): 248 scenarios, 544 dispatcher calls, 4,042 arm entries, 3,130 row visits, 792 table comparisons (every byte of the original's first ten timer records against the native host's), 0 mismatches, 61 of 61 native mutants caught. 5 C unit cases (ASan/UBSan clean) with 24 unit mutants, a corpus driver over all 32 real rows, an executor test (an open row revisited, a gated clear) with one mutant. The full suite on Linux, the comparison against plain main and the independent review are recorded in the PR description.

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud). (2) Native safety differences: a timer index outside 0..9 (signed word in the original) and a seconds word the table host cannot source are refused UNSUPPORTED before any effect; the oracle sources the table words 5000..17685 and does not test beyond. (3) Readers of the timer record (condition leaves, the clock, the task list UI, the sound named by the record text) are other slices, so a configured timer does nothing visible yet. (4) The oracle builds the rows in memory (the scene loader `0x40b34a` is not run); the corpus rows' dependency tails are replayed behind stand-in records.

Next: 0x4b (5 scenes, 9 records), 0x1b (4), 0x7d (4) by `scene-action-coverage.json`; raw 0x2d is RT-36; porting the save routine `0x4d7a15`.

## Handoff: RT-36 the raw 0x2d random-movie set runtime and the raw 0x2e mode 0 stop (2026-10-16 PDT, cloud thread)

Merged in this slice: raw 0x2d (CRandomMovie at 0x63821d, handler 0x4cd6ff phases 0 to 2): `src/action_family_2d_runtime.[ch]` (set parser, clip load with the four `.BIK`/`.AVF` probes, AVF and Bink steps, weighted next-clip pick 0x4cf0ce, stop 0x4ceb3b, destructor 0x4cd218, render entry 0x4cecf7 and render plan 0x4cf749) and `src/session_bindings_a2d.[ch]`. The random set name and every entry lookup use the CRT `_stricmp` (0x516530), found by the oracle. The object is destroyed and its render entry registered once at creation (the original does both at world init 0x4381f4, not at a scene load, found in review); a scene load while a movie is active is refused with UNSUPPORTED, because what the original does to a live movie's tables is not recovered. Raw 0x2e mode 0 now runs the stop: the 0x2d row is erased and completed through the new `scene_executor_retire_action` / `scene_session_retire_action`, the start flag is applied, and the 0x2e row itself stays pending (the original's completion store lands on row -1), so it is visited again.

Scene counts (`scene-action-coverage.json`): scenes with every action type handled (type-level upper bound) went from 2,024 (after RT-39) to 2,027 of 2,078 (+3, the three scenes whose only missing type was 0x2d). Those three are bound but still blocked in the app: phase 0 is refused until a media backend exists. Ported-function count unchanged (231).

Verification: Unicorn oracle `tests/action_family_2d_runtime_original_oracle.py` runs the real dispatcher 0x427a50 and the unmodified object code against the kernel: 640 scenarios, 24,935 steps, 11,135 handler calls, 670,609 object field compares, 0 mismatches, 62 of 62 planted mutants caught; the one kernel fix it forced is the `_stricmp` compare. Expected differences are tagged (zero divisor faults in the original, native returns an error; the destructor leaves 0x5d1a45 to the binding). 12 C unit cases clean under ASan/UBSan plus 14 source mutants (and one on the corpus driver), an executor test of 0x2e mode 0 against the production executor with 3 mutants, a corpus driver over the 3 real rows (2095, 2184, 2643: subtype 1, buffer ring, 89 entries). The 0x2e oracle was re-run: 0 mismatches, 36 of 36 mutants caught.

**Not verified.** `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud; binding 33 of 36). The phase-2 stop of a 0x2d row completes it directly and does not reset the row's dependency state (the 0x2e-driven stop does). Most kernel world inputs (scene key word 0x5bb50b, view origin, thread argument, back buffer, several globals) are not mapped in the app glue; they must be before `media_available` can return 1. The app has no random-movie media backend (no AVF decode thread, no Bink player binding, no clip file probes), so `media_available` is 0 and phase 0 of a raw 0x2d row reports an UNSUPPORTED blocker before any effect: the 3 scenes still do not play their movie, they stop at that row. The locale-aware `_stricmp` path is not modelled (C locale only). The decode thread procedure 0x4cf687 and the pixel copies are host behaviour, driven by scripted values in the oracle.

Next: a media backend for the random movie (AVF decode thread, Bink player binding, file probes) so phase 0 can run in the app; Mac build of the glue; the remaining ranked runtime families.

## Handoff: RT-42 raw 0x47 journal entry add runtime (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Packet `tasks/RT-42-journal-entry-add-47-runtime.md`. Raw 0x47 (loader `0x411a22`, arm `0x428515` of `0x427a50`, gate mode 0, appender `0x412787`) now runs natively: `src/action_family_47_runtime.[ch]` (kernel over the three journal tables), `src/session_bindings_a47.[ch]` (binding that owns the tables and the page-2 "new entry" flags), app glue in `src/blackmoor_app.mm` as `bindings[38]`, count 39, module pair in `scripts/build_native_app.sh`. Raw 0x7d (RT-41) merged first with slot 37.

What it does: the body is kind (3, 4 or 5 picks the table), a 33-byte name, a code word and a link-value word. The visit ends without an add when a stored entry has the same name, or when the AUTOTEXT lookup of the name misses (the original repeats that lookup once per non-matching stored entry); otherwise the entry is added (strcpy, code, link value kept for code >= 10 or 7 else 9999, count + 1) and kinds 4 and 3 set the page-2 flag dwords. These tables are the link tables the text writer fills (table 0), so this unblocks the journal pages.

Scene counts (`scene-action-coverage.json`): scenes with every action type handled (type-level upper bound) 2,035 (after RT-41) to 2,038 of 2,078 (+3; the fourth raw 0x47 scene also needs another type). Ported-function count unchanged (231).
