## Handoff: RT-44 raw 0x4e float value recomputation runtime (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Packet `tasks/RT-44-float-value-recompute-4e-runtime.md`. Raw 0x4e (loader `0x44b102`, arm `0x4280f9` of `0x427a50`, gate mode 1, method `0x44b3c8`) now runs natively: `src/action_family_4e_runtime.[ch]` (kernel over the `ActionCoreWorld` value slots, using the A-4E.1 decoder), `src/session_bindings_a4e.[ch]` (binding: COMPLETED, phase 0), app glue in `src/blackmoor_app.mm` as `bindings[39]`, count 40, module trio in `scripts/build_native_app.sh`.

Sizing of the four ranked families (asm lines of the arm's method): 0x4e 268; 0x33 678 (three phases, a 0x46-byte player object built through a constructor and virtual calls, a hotspot table, video surfaces, about fifteen unported callees, so even a faithful port would stop at the media player in the app); 0xea 1,122; 0xf6 2,387 (both draw through large helper sets). Only 0x4e was slice-sized; 0x33, 0xea and 0xf6 are puzzle-class and stay open (3 scenes each).

What it does: the body is a target byte (30..39 = the ten float value slots) and ten (selector, amount) triples. The target slot is cleared, then each triple adds a term: selector 99 none, 0..29 `amount / 100 * int16 slot`, 30..39 the same with a float slot, 100 the amount; a slot holding the 9999 / 9999.0f "unset" marker (or NaN) contributes 0; any other selector is reported and contributes 0. A target outside 30..39 changes nothing. Every path completes the row. The x87 arithmetic (53-bit) is reproduced exactly: `(float)((double)(float)amount / 100.0 * slot)` per term and `(float)((double)term + (double)target)` per sum.

Scene counts (`scene-action-coverage.json`): scenes with every action type handled (type-level upper bound) 2,038 (after RT-42) to 2,041 of 2,078 (+3; all three raw 0x4e scenes had it as their only missing type). Ported-function count unchanged (231).

Verification: original-x86 oracle `tests/action_family_4e_runtime_original_oracle.py` runs the real loader `0x44b102`, dispatcher `0x427a50` and method `0x44b3c8` under Unicorn (x87 control word 0x27f) from random value states (unset markers, NaN, infinities, denormals, signed zeros, random bit patterns): 288 scenarios, 940 ticks, 3,090 visited rows, 13,272 terms (693 unset), 16,066 float cells written, 1,247 selector reports, 940 whole-value-area compares (160 bytes each), 1,414 loader objects, 0 mismatches; 39 of 39 planted native mutants caught. Ten rounding witnesses (amount, slot pairs where `a * slot / 100` rounds to a different float than `a / 100 * slot`) pin the order of the original's fdiv and fmul. 10 C unit cases (ASan/UBSan clean) with 20 kernel and 6 binding source mutants, an executor test with 2 mutants, a corpus driver over the 18 real rows (3 scenes). Full suite and review results are in the PR.

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud); only the new handler was syntax-checked against stand-in types. (2) The debug flag `0x63a4cd` is modelled as 0 (the original logs on an out-of-range target when it is set). (3) The out-of-range selector report (`0x52139c`, a modal error in the original) is only logged in the app. (4) The scene loader `0x40b34a` is not run (rows are built in memory around the original `0x44b102`); the corpus rows' real dependency records are replayed behind the stand-in event record. (5) An invalid-operation NaN (0 * infinity) keeps the x87 sign on the Intel oracle host but is the arm64 default NaN natively; unreachable with finite game data. (6) The three real scenes use only float-slot and literal selectors against unset slots, so what the game's 18 rows produce depends on how earlier actions set the slots; the short-slot branch is covered by the oracle but not by real rows.

Next: 0x33 (3 scenes, needs the media player host), 0xea (3), 0xf6 (3), then the 2-scene families (0x1c, 0x20, 0x70, 0x77, 0x80, 0xd0, 0xd3, 0xe0, 0xe4) by `scene-action-coverage.json`.

## Handoff: RT-46 raw 0x33 secondary video with hotspots runtime (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Merged as https://github.com/albovsky/nd-blackmoor-decomp/pull/109 (2026-10-16, ~9:05 PM PDT). Map: `/mnt/project-files/progress-map-after-rt46.png` (functions ported 231 of 4,188, unchanged).

Packet `tasks/RT-46-secondary-video-33-runtime.md`. Raw 0x33 (loader arm `0x40c186`, dispatcher arm of `0x427a50`, gate mode 1, handler `0x42c221`) now runs natively: `src/action_family_33_runtime.[ch]` (parser, `A33State` process globals, `a33_step` with phases 0, 1, 2 and the pure callees `0x42ca22` event flags, `0x42e72c` page flip, `0x42d215` frame advance, `0x42caa8` + `0x42cecd` hotspot rectangles), `src/session_bindings_a33.[ch]` (binding, pre-gate request clear, pointer row), app glue in `src/blackmoor_app.mm` as `bindings[40]`, count 41, module pair in `scripts/build_native_app.sh`, `scripts/build_action_family_33_runtime.sh`. (Slot number is 40 in this branch; whichever of RT-45 and RT-46 merges second renumbers.)

What it does: phase 0 tears down a previous reader thread, buffers and surfaces, creates the "SEC_0_VIDEO_0" surface and the reader thread, picks the start frame by the animation word, then falls into the play pass. Phase 1 waits for the reader's ready word, then on the first hotspot whose key equals the selector runs the event flags, page flip, frame advance and rectangles and arms a tick deadline; after that it re-runs the rectangles every pass and the flip/flags/advance whenever a frame is flagged and the deadline passed. Phase 2 (set by the executor on a hotspot click) returns until a frame is flagged, then pushes the saved-scene record (body byte +0x6c), stores the scene-change cells and engine mode 4 and completes the row (`0x42ba3a`). Every media call (thread, surfaces, stream lock/reset, Z list, buffers, tick) is one `A33Op` of the host in the original order; the reader thread proc `0x44cde2` is host behaviour.

Scene counts (`scene-action-coverage.json`): scenes with every action type handled (type-level upper bound) 2,041 to 2,044 of 2,078 (+3, all three raw 0x33 scenes). Ported-function count unchanged (231).

Verification: original-x86 oracle `tests/action_family_33_runtime_original_oracle.py` (+ `_oracle_driver.c`, written by a Sonnet worker, rerun by the thread): real loader `0x40b34a`, real dispatcher `0x427a50` and gate, the six handler functions as unhooked original x86 (1,045 of 1,051 instructions reached), every media call a hook mapped to one op: 1,100 scenarios, 3,798 steps, 6,471 ops, 201,294 state-field and 22,944 row-cell comparisons, 27 fatal steps matching, 0 mismatches; 86 of 86 planted native mutants caught. Unit: 14 kernel cases groups plus binding cases (`tests/action_family_33_runtime_test.c`, `_binding.c`), 27 kernel and 7 binding source mutants caught, UBSan/ASan clean.

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud); the new block was syntax-checked against stand-in types only. (2) The app has no media backend, so phase 0 is refused as UNSUPPORTED (as raw 0x2d): the three scenes still stop at this row in the app. `avf_player` (SECONDARY_VIDEO) models the reader thread and is not wired. (3) The pointer cascade's own handling of the playing secondary video (`0x4f1e00..0x4f2563`: it sets the animation state 1 and frame `+0x50` on a click) is not ported, so the hold/reverse animation never starts natively. (4) Inputs without an app counterpart are left zero: clip rectangle `0x5bb583..`, video rectangle `0x5b4de8..`, thread argument `0x5b016f`, candidate `0x5c39e5`, ready word, delay, decode buffers. (5) `session_a33_begin_scene` runs `a33_scene_clear`, the 0x33 part of the scene clear `0x43dbe3` (latch `0x5c39f1`, words `0x5b4e89`/`0x5b4e8b`/`0x5b4e8d`, `0x5d1967`, `0x5c39e5`, surfaces `0x5d03c7` and `0x5d03bf`); it was read from the disassembly and is not run in the oracle. A scene whose 0x33 rows fail to parse leaves no 0x33 row owned. The reader thread and decode buffers stay for the next phase 0. (6) Debug flag `0x63a4cd` modelled as 0; logging calls are only recorded. (7) Native safety differences tagged in the oracle: negative counts, unterminated names, short bodies, a hotspot-cell write for a row other than `0x5b4e89`, host failure. (8) Only 7 oracle handler visits ran behind the corpus rows' real dependency records; the gate is shared verified code. (9) The oracle's mutants are exact-text anchors into the kernel and report UNAPPLIED if it is reformatted.

Next: media backend (AVF decode thread / Bink player) shared by raw 0x2d and 0x33; cascade case for the secondary video; then 0xea (RT-45), 0xf6, and the 2-scene families (0x1c, 0x20, 0x70, 0x77, 0x80, 0xd0, 0xd3, 0xe0, 0xe4, 0xeb) by `scene-action-coverage.json`.

(6) `+0x2a9` (draw) is only written by a dead compare in the original, so the draw block is unreachable in play. (7) The host `random` callback is not validated for negative values (the app masks to 0x7fff).

## Handoff: RT-53 raw 0xd3 Collision Puzzle runtime (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Packet `tasks/RT-53-collision-puzzle-d3-runtime.md`. Ranking after PR 117: 0x1c, 0xd3, 0xe4, 0xeb, 0xf5 each unblock two scenes. Raw 0xd3 has the smallest engine-free closure (about 7,000 unique bytes: handler `0x463392`, pointer `0x462c39`, move step `0x461d82`, grid setup `0x462577`, move check `0x4619be`, render `0x463b10`; no random numbers, no text drawing). 0xe4 (Match Puzzle) needs `_rand`, a score table and text drawing (about 12,700 bytes); 0xeb is about twice the size; 0x1c reads files and drives an external player. Raw 0xd3 is the "Collision Puzzle": pieces slide cell by cell in one direction (left 0x10, up 0x11, down 0x12, right 0x13) until a barrier, wall (type 0x14) or another piece stops them; the puzzle is solved when every piece rests on the cell type equal to its number; solving plays a final sound after a delay and leaves through the solved scene block, the cancel rectangle leaves through the cancel block. The grid (8 x 8 cells, 40 bytes each at `0x594db5`, setup flag `0x594db3`) persists across visits unless body byte 0x21 is 0.

The character path forwards keys whenever the quiz is active, without the UI-modality checks the original message handler `0x437e92` may make (unverified). Native-only refusals (not run against the original): body validation (type count outside 0..20, piece size outside 1..4096, unterminated fields, animation area with zero columns), phase 1 or 2 without phase 0, hand or list index outside the instances, instance type byte outside 0..19, more than 20 pieces under the pointer, event flag index outside the host window, spawning an 81st instance, captions longer than the 204-byte (click) or 208-byte (win) local, the z-level render loop guard of 0x4000.

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud); it was syntax-checked against stand-in types. (2) Sounds are logged "NOT PLAYED" and report not busy, so the win sound wait finishes at once. (3) Blits are plain; the background role uses the back-buffer recompose. (4) The second mouse button does not exist in the app yet (logged "SECOND BUTTON NOT AVAILABLE"), so piece rotation by button is untested live; rotation by zone click works. (5) The second exit button is empty (both rects zero) in both real rows. (6) The saved scene word `0x5a0561` is also written by the class at `0x47a416`; that coupling is not modelled. (7) The saved-state compare in the piece builder runs after the handler overwrote the saved scene (original quirk, preserved): with the persist byte set, a saved flag and a different scene the records stay zero. (8) The kernel's `top_piece` does not validate raw list entries (reachable only through host corruption). (9) The oracle hooks (surface, blit, sound, text, slot machinery) are scripted; only their call order and arguments are compared. (10) Reviewer P2, not fixed (kernel change would invalidate the recorded oracle run): the native pick-limit refusal (more than 20 pieces under the pointer) IS reachable in play, by badly dropping a spawn-type piece 20 times (each failed drop leaves one more unstamped copy at home; the original overflows its 20-word list deterministically, native refuses and the glue discards the error); the oracle docstring's "unreachable in the game" for the native-only refusals is therefore wrong for this one. Native refusals also leave partial writes (for example `O_DRAG` set before an instance check in `eb_click`, sounds replayed when a caption refuses in state 6); all need host input beyond what the game supplies. A follow-up should saturate the list or mirror the overwrite. (11) `phase0` leaks the piece surfaces if slot allocation fails and the row is retried.

Independent review: one Sonnet `slice-reviewer` read the diff only; see the PR.

Next: remaining families 0x1c (reads files, external player), 0x76, 0xda, 0xee (one scene each); the app gaps (media backend, right and second mouse button input, sound wiring, text drawing, 0x44 link table, save routine).

## Handoff: RT-57 raw 0x9a, 0x6d and 0x1c runtimes (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Packet `tasks/RT-57-sound-stop-9a-popup-close-6d-interactive-video-1c-runtime.md`. After PR 120 eight scenes still had a type without a handler. Ranking by closure size (direct-call closure over game functions): 0x9a 8 functions / 2.1 KB and 0x6d about 20 / 3.2 KB (together S6301, one scene), 0x1c 24 / 4.2 KB (S2186 and S3420), 0x76 63 / 30 KB (S4160), 0xda 100 / 46 KB (S1261, two rows), 0xee 104 / 46 KB (S1206, two rows); 0xeb (S2348, S3600) is RT-56's. This slice took the three small ones. Scenes with every action type handled: 2,075 of 2,078 after merging RT-56's 0xeb (PR 121).

What the originals are. Raw 0x9a (handler `0x4336ad`, arm `0x429d34`): phase 0 sets state 2; phase 2 stops sound channels (`0x14` = channels 0..13, `0x15` = 1..13, else that channel; `0x4fc878` on `0x5c3a67`), optionally takes the 0x0a-style scene-change cells (word 1 != 9999) with start flag = (word at bytes 25..26 != 1) and engine mode 4, stores an event flag, completes directly. Raw 0x6d (arm `0x4290c0`): picks a popup teardown from `[0x5bb22a]` (overlay `0x4e303b`) / `[0x583204]` 4 (`0x4eb96e`) / 3 (`0x4e7d04`), unloads the scene sound `0x4fb018`, stores outer state 6 and three zero cells, completes directly. Raw 0x1c (handler `0x4cb586`, singleton CInteractiveVideo `0x63847b`, gate mode 1): loads a `.IV` file (33-byte name, entry count, 13-byte entries each followed by 24-byte hotspot rectangles) through the CRT, waits until the movie player `0x637fcf` is in state 2 with the same clip name, registers a z entry (label `INTERACTIVE VIDEO DIAGS`), follows the player's hotspot index (translating the matching entry's rectangles by the player's origin once per change) and frees everything when the player leaves state 2/3; its render callback `0x4cc79a` fills each rectangle in a unique colour (looks like a hidden hit map).

What landed: `src/action_family_{9a,6d,1c}_runtime.[ch]`, `src/session_bindings_a6d_a9a.[ch]`, `src/session_bindings_a1c.[ch]`, `scripts/build_action_family_6d_9a_runtime.sh`, `scripts/build_action_family_1c_runtime.sh`, modules in `scripts/build_native_app.sh`, and the glue in `blackmoor_app.mm`: **binding slots 55 (0x6d), 56 (0x9a), 57 (0x1c), declared count 58** (RT-56's 0xeb holds 54; RT-58 plans 58 and renumbers if it merges second: a gap or a duplicate breaks `scene_session_create` and the map script); `a1c_slot_used` was added to all eleven z entry allocators.

Verification (all in the cloud, against the real GAME.EXE under Unicorn): `tests/action_family_6d_9a_runtime_original_oracle.py` 574 scenarios, 1,177 ticks, 89,632 compared fields, 0 mismatches, 86 native-only refusal checks, mutation check 190 of 190 counted mutants killed (10 proved equivalent). `tests/action_family_1c_runtime_original_oracle.py` 282 scenarios, 1,698 ticks, 58,771 compared fields, 0 mismatches, 109 native-only refusal checks, mutation check 383 of 383 counted mutants killed (27 equivalent, reasons in the script). The 1c oracle found four real defects in the first kernel (phase 2 left the phase at 2 instead of 0; the binding returned COMPLETED, which leaves the completion byte to the subtype, where the original sets it directly, so every subtype but 2 is DIRECT_COMPLETED now; the body pointer was not stored on early returns; a 34-byte name buffer overflowed on long names), all fixed. Unit tests: `tests/action_family_6d_9a_runtime_test.c` (18 cases) and `tests/action_family_1c_runtime_test.c` (29 cases), ASan/UBSan clean, source-mutant sweeps of the kernels and bindings (158 and 295 mutants, all killed, equivalents recorded), production-executor drivers and corpus drivers for the real rows (S6301: 2 rows; S2186 and S3420: both subtype 2, 105-byte bodies, render word 0 so no z entry). Ported-function count unchanged (231 of 4,188); ledger, evidence and scene coverage regenerated.

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain); syntax-checked against stand-in types only. (2) 0x1c in the app only loads its `.IV` file and then waits in phase 0 forever: the movie player at `0x637fcf` is not wired (the app plays scene movies through Bink on its own path), the render callback is a logged stub (no hit-map surface), the `.IV` path rule (`<game dir>/Ciftree/<name>.IV`, case-insensitive; `0x639db1` is the CifTreePath setting default `\Ciftree\`) is inferred from `0x4348b0` and not run on a real game directory, and the real `.IV` files are game data that are not in the cloud, so the file layout was only checked against the original handler with synthetic files. The other CInteractiveVideo methods (`0x4cb058`, `0x4cb3ab`, `0x4cbf72`, `0x4cc3bd`, probably click and pointer handling) are not ported. (3) 0x6d: the popup teardown functions' callees (the inventory/journal/overlay screens) belong to the host; the app has no popup screens, so its cells stay 0 and only the tail runs, and a nonzero cell is refused as UNSUPPORTED; the oracle verifies the wrappers' direct cell stores but not the conditional image releases inside them. (4) 0x9a: channels inside the live audio table are stopped through `live_audio_stop_channel` (0x4fc878 keeps the channel loaded), others are logged NOT STOPPED; a refused scene-change request leaves the start flag already stored; the raw 0x9b arm (same function with unload) is not registered because no scene uses it. (5) The kernel reads each hotspot rectangle with its own 24-byte read where the original makes one read of count * 24; outcomes are identical. (6) A power-on 0x1c object has slot -1 and last index -1, as the constructor 0x4caed0 leaves it (the oracle checks this), so a phase 2 visit without phase 0 releases nothing; not reachable through the executor anyway.

Blockers left (3 scenes): 0xee (S1206, 104 functions / 46 KB), 0xda (S1261, 100 / 46 KB) and 0x76 (S4160, 63 / 30 KB) can be ported in the cloud but each is a multi-slice effort; none is blocked by missing cloud evidence. Next after those: the app gaps (movie backend, which would also let 0x1c, 0x2d and 0x33 run, right mouse button, sound wiring, text drawing, save routine).
## Handoff: RT-58 raw 0x76 PASSWORD puzzle runtime (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Packet `tasks/RT-58-password-puzzle-76-runtime.md`. After RT-56 the action types without a handler were 0x1c, 0x6d, 0x9a (RT-57's three), 0x76, 0xda and 0xee (one scene each). Raw 0x76 is the smallest: one handler `0x42fc70` (3,858 bytes) with no engine helper beyond font, sound and text width. It is the PASSWORD puzzle (resource 2529 action 7, one 575-byte row, one text field, three accepted words): the player types into one or two text fields with a blinking '-' cursor and presses Enter; the typed text (upper case) must contain an accepted word (upper case, `strstr`); a right answer plays the success sound and leaves through the correct scene block, a wrong one plays the wrong sound and leaves through the wrong block, a click on the cancel strip (not shifted by the view origin) leaves through the cancel block. Handler states 0..5 on `0x5b61b4`, character receiver `0x437dcd` (filter `0x437ea1`), pointer block `0x4f04b6..0x4f05a3` of the cascade `0x4f0059`, click block of `0x43a7ca`, invalidate `0x43d7f0`, cleanup `0x43dbe3`, new game `0x435015`, render `0x4d07e7` (static z entry 22, flag `0x5d1c01`).

The binding allocates no z slot (the render entry is static), so no other allocator changed. Scenes with every action type handled: 2,076 of 2,078. Ported-function count unchanged (231 of 4,188; ledger, evidence and scene coverage regenerated).

Verification: original-x86 oracle `tests/action_family_76_runtime_original_oracle.py` (real dispatcher `0x427a50` with gate and generic completion under Unicorn; the handler, render callback, key function, pointer / click / invalidate / cleanup / new-game blocks run as original code on one image; `python3 action_family_76_runtime_original_oracle.py --workers 4 --result <path>` from `tests/`, about 9 s): 401 scenarios, 22,906 dispatcher ticks, 110,939 ordered events, 6,920,890 compared fields, all 256 filter bytes three times, 364 native refusal checks, 0 mismatches; handler visits phase 1 states 0..5 10,874 / 1,511 / 243 / 2,810 / 5,568 / 122, phase 2 cancel 147 / correct 50 / wrong 120; UBSan run of the driver also 0 mismatches. The oracle found one kernel deviation, fixed: a hit in field 1 when the body has a second field only moves to state 1 (the original sets the correct flag only without a second field or in the field 2 loop). Mutation check `tests/action_family_76_runtime_mutation_check.py`: 889 kernel and header mutants, 50 invalid or rejected by the strict build, 839 counted, 812 killed, 27 equivalent with reasons in the script, 0 open. Unit tests `tests/action_family_76_runtime_test.c` (18 cases, plain and ASan/UBSan clean), `tests/test_action_family_76_runtime.py` (427 source mutants of kernel and binding, all killed, 12 recorded equivalent) and `tests/action_family_76_runtime_corpus.c` (the real row: wrong exit, three accepted words in 9 spellings, correct exit, cancel, blink, Backspace; derived counts only).

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain in the cloud); the 0x76 blocks were syntax-checked against stand-in types, the one-line additions (forward declarations, character hook, cleanup, new game) were not. (2) The text width `0x4d3836` is a scripted function shared by the oracle and the native host; the app uses `caption_internal_text_width` (verified by the caption slices) with an 8 pixel per character fallback. (3) No text is drawn (the kernel emits the text ops, the glue logs "PASSWORD TEXT NOT DRAWN" once), so the player cannot see what they type until the app has a positioned text draw. (4) Sounds are logged "NOT PLAYED" and report not busy, so the sound waits finish at once. (5) The case conversion `0x50d0bc` is taken in the C locale (`0x63cb1c == 0`); the `LCMapStringA` branch is not covered. (6) Replaced in the oracle: font acquire / release, text width, the five sound routines, blit `0x4c5377` and text draw `0x4d2c20` (arguments compared, pixels not), GetTickCount; `0x4a1f59`, `0x48e93e`, `0x4b8193` (other puzzles called by `0x437dcd`) are stubs. Block boundaries of the click, invalidate, cleanup and new-game blocks are read from the disassembly. (7) Layer order of the background restore blit against the UI, the cursor sprite cells and the engine mode := 4 are logged or unverified in the app, as for the other puzzles. (8) The cursor blink depends on the app clock advancing every tick. (9) A field with 33 non-NUL bytes would run on in the original; the kernel truncates (unreachable through the game). (10) `session_a76_invalidate` (0x43d7f0) is landed and tested but the app does not call it (phase 0 sets the entry flag and phase 2 or the scene clear resets it, so nothing is lost). The UBSan runs are clean over the generated rectangle ranges; the review found a signed overflow in the width limit for a field of width 0x80000000, fixed with unsigned arithmetic. (11) Slots 0..58 are all filled (gap-free) after merging main.

Next: remaining types 0xda and 0xee (one scene each; their handlers `0x45c18f` (4,343 bytes) and `0x47c0f0` (3,212 bytes) pull in about six helpers each), RT-57's 0x1c, 0x6d, 0x9a, and the app gaps (media backend, right and second mouse button input, sound wiring, text drawing for 0xe4 / 0x44 / 0x76, 0x44 link table, save routine).

Unit tests `tests/action_family_da_runtime_test.c` (31 cases, plain and ASan/UBSan clean) and `tests/test_action_family_da_runtime.py` (source-mutant sweep of kernel and binding: 770 of 770 runnable mutants killed, 18 equivalent with reasons, 6 sanitizer-only killed under ASan), `tests/action_family_da_runtime_corpus.c` runs both real rows through the binding (56 games, 29 won and 19 lost out of 48 played, 8 left through the finish rectangle; derived counts only).

(2) Sounds are logged "NOT PLAYED" and report not busy, so every caption wait ends at once. (4) The render draws opaque copies except the button-down blit (the only `0x4c5d4b` call in `0x45d2c6`, colour keyed); layer order against the UI is unverified. ## Handoff: RT-60 raw 0xee Memory puzzle runtime (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Packet `tasks/RT-60-memory-puzzle-ee-runtime.md`. Raw 0xee is the Memory puzzle (CMemoryPuzzle, resource 926 actions 1 and 2, S1206): a grid of face-down tiles (one to three pages of up to 6 x 4, page tabs optional) hides pairs of pictures; the player flips two tiles, a pair stays up, a mismatch turns back after the body's delay (and plays a "wrong" sound at once); enough pairs within the allowed tries wins, running out of tries loses; the win or lose scene block, event flag and generic completion follow a win / lose sound. Handler `0x47c0f0` (dispatcher arm `0x42a5f9`, ECX `0x61e398`, gate `0x423f57` mode 0), pointer `0x47b4aa` (cascade call `0x4f0fb4`), click `0x47b767` (accepted-release call `0x43b3a9`, acts on the candidates `0x2768` page tab and `0x2769` tile), invalidate `0x47b3bc`, clear `0x47b321` (scene cleanup, new game and handler phase 2), render `0x47d24e` (z entry type 0xb), deal `0x47bb56` with `_rand`, sound-record player `0x47d02d`.

What landed: `src/action_family_ee_runtime.[ch]` (kernel, prefix `Mp`; the object is kept as the raw 0x2c4-byte image plus the two heap blocks, whose guest addresses `0x30000000` / `0x30010000` the oracle's allocator hook returns), `src/session_bindings_aee.[ch]`, `scripts/build_action_family_ee_runtime.sh`, `scripts/build_native_app.sh` module list, the glue in `blackmoor_app.mm` (**binding slot 59, declared count 60**: RT-59 (0xda) was still unmerged when this PR was made and also plans slot 59, the slots must stay gap-free, so whichever merges second renumbers; every other z-slot allocator now excludes aee and aee excludes them). Scenes with every action type handled: 2,077 of 2,078 (0xda in S1261 is the last). Ported-function count unchanged (231 of 4,188; ledger, evidence and scene coverage regenerated).

Verification: original-x86 oracle `tests/action_family_ee_runtime_original_oracle.py` (real dispatcher `0x427a50` with gate and generic completion under Unicorn; pointer, click, invalidate, cleanup, render and the deal / sound helpers run as original code; `python3 action_family_ee_runtime_original_oracle.py --workers 4 --result <path>` from `tests/`, about 40 s): 204 scenarios (9 real-body, 140 random, 55 synthetic), 5,543 ticks, 4,075 handler steps, 7,787 clicks, 10,704 pointer moves, 241 renders (1,670 blits), 25,864 host calls, 24,879 state compares, 9,162 `_rand` draws, 0 mismatches; the one fatal path of the original (z slots full) matched; 129 native-only checks (validation, bounds, stuck loops, host failure injection). The oracle proved no kernel difference, so the kernel is exactly the owner draft. Unit tests `tests/action_family_ee_runtime_test.c` (26 cases, plain and ASan/UBSan clean), `tests/test_action_family_ee_runtime.py` (source mutants of kernel and binding: 776 killed, 37 recorded equivalent with reasons, 2 named open gaps in the binding guard `present == 0u || index > INT16_MAX` that only a scene with more than 32,767 rows reaches) and `tests/action_family_ee_runtime_corpus.c` (the two real rows: validate, deal, flip pairs and mismatches with the flip-back delay, win exit; derived counts only).

**Not verified, honest limits.** (1) `blackmoor_app.mm` glue was not compiled (no macOS toolchain); syntax-checked against stand-in types only. (2) Oracle mutation check `tests/action_family_ee_runtime_mutation_check.py`: 1,544 mutants, 1,497 counted, 1,218 killed on the quick scenario subset, 9 equivalent with reasons, 270 unsettled survivors; only 46 of the quick survivors were re-run on the full 204 scenarios (11 killed), so the true kill rate is above the reported 81.85% by an unknown amount. Finishing it is a resumable run (`python3 action_family_ee_runtime_mutation_check.py --stage quick --jobs 4`, about 2 hours). The likely killable survivors are the id / needed clamp edges, tile-shrink edge pixels and all-"NO SOUND" lists. (3) The deal's float scaling (`/ 100.0f * float(n)`, truncated) is evaluated in double precision, as under the 0x027f x87 control word; the ambient control word was never proven, and the oracle's percentages exercise it with few values. (4) The oracle never uses pages 0 or 4 and above, so the kernel's stricter validation (the original's signed checks accept them and then misbehave) is checked natively only. (5) Captions of 98 or more characters run past the original's 104-byte local (it corrupts its frame from about 102 characters); the kernel refuses 98 and more natively (conservative; the real rows have no captions). (6) The cursor recenter `0x4db870` (real data sets body +0x345) is a host call; the app logs "CURSOR RECENTER NOT PERFORMED". Sounds are logged "NOT PLAYED" and report not busy, so the win / lose waits finish at once. (7) The oracle run with UBSan was not done. (8) The original frees the cell array and id table in the clear; the kernel keeps their bytes (the oracle compares them only while the object's pointers are nonzero). The original writes body +0x346 := 0 for one page; the kernel uses the forced value without writing. (9) `session_aee_registered` stays 1 after a failing finish (the entry is not released on that path). (10) Review (Sonnet, diff and disassembly, no P1): a click that fails after the cell is marked face up (a host error or an over-long caption) leaves that cell face up and unpaired; the header says the session must stop on a host failure, the app only logs it. Not reachable with the real rows (no captions).

Next: every action type now has a handler; the app gaps (movie backend, right mouse button, sound wiring, positioned text drawing for 0xe4 / 0x44 / 0x76, sprite transparency, 0x44 link table, save routine).

## Handoff: FL-10 runtime oracle evidence into the function ledger (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Why the ported count stayed at 231 after the runtime slices: (1) `function-execution.json` (FL-8) held only 96 of the 148 oracle scripts; none of the RT-23..RT-59 runtime oracles had been recorded, so no handler ever had an `executed` oracle window. (2) Even with a window, the ledger credits one original function per native symbol: `port_acceptances` needs a native symbol whose body or leading comment holds the VA, a test that names it, and a per-function review. The runtime kernels inline most original helpers into one phase handler per family, so that mapping is usually missing.

What landed: 38 more oracle runs recorded (96 -> 134) in the cloud (Unicorn, Linux-built libraries through a stand-in `xcrun`, `pip install unicorn capstone pefile`), merged into `function-execution.json` with the new `record_function_execution.py collect --merge --write` (the Mac traces of the other 96 runs are not at hand; 134 runs, 611 functions executed, was 96 and 366). Evidence and ledger regenerated. 98 pending functions then had a fully executed window, a native symbol and a test; five Sonnet reviewers read each against the disassembly and the oracle script, and 19 were accepted as `native_oracle_verified` after the diff review dropped `0x44d597` (EB 7; 1c, 76, aa 2, d0, d1, d8, e4 4, ea: 12); 79 were rejected (41 inlined in a larger native symbol or cited only in a comment, 15 oracle does not compare that function, 6 no test names the symbol, rest wrong mapping or fragment). Ported 231 -> 250 of 4,188.

Not recorded (need Mac game data or longer): `avf_decoder`, `avf_player`, `his_vorbis_decoder`, `caption_layout`, `caption_lifecycle`, `live_audio_restart`, `startup`, `condition_extra` oracles (game data or Mac frameworks), `action_family_d9_runtime` (timed out after 50 min), `action_family_f6_runtime` (corpus missing), `action_family_e0_runtime`/`action_family_ee_runtime`/`world_init` (not run in this pass), `matching_s08_2` (excluded by design); 14 scripts in all. Still pending with a fully executed window but no 1:1 native symbol or test: 230 game functions (plus 21 library ones, which never count). Crediting those needs a rule change (see the thread reply).

## Handoff: FL-11 direct tests for helpers the native kernels already cite, 30 more ported (2026-10-17 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **280 ported, 128 not needed, 3,780 pending** (was 250 after FL-10, PR 127). All 30 are `native_oracle_verified`: the native symbol already named the original address in its body or leading comment and an unhooked, fully executed oracle window existed; only a test that names the symbol was missing, so no `src/` file changed.

How: of the 230 executed-but-uncredited rows in FL-10's list, the 50 whose native candidate is a `src/*.c` symbol with a VA citation and no named test went to two Sonnet readers (pseudocode and disassembly against the native body and the oracle script). 32 accepted, 18 rejected; I dropped 2 of the 32 (`0x4b8a27` more_balloons and `0x4b8d5e` expire_balloons: when the count is low the original leaves a local uninitialised, native uses 0, the oracle zeroes the stack so the comparison cannot tell, and that branch is reachable in the game). Five Sonnet implementers wrote one C test per original function (`tests/fl11_<family>_direct_test.c` `#include`s the kernel `.c` and calls the static helper, in the style of `action_family_28_x87_division_fixture.c`; `tests/test_fl11_<family>_direct.py` builds and runs it with a 60 s timeout). Expected values come from the original's pseudocode, not from the kernel; about 90 planted mutants were all caught (equivalent ones were identified and noted). One Sonnet diff reviewer found no P1; its P2s (truncated notes, missing timeouts) and P3 (helper functions as test pointers) were fixed.

Accepted: 1b `0x414947` action1b_step; 33 `0x42cecd` second_rects, `0x42d215` frame_advance; aa `0x44d946` write_alarm; da `0x45ba0b` reset_work, `0x45bcc1` step_check, `0x45be7f` caption_pick; d3 `0x461d4b` type_is, `0x462577` grid_setup, `0x462b84` cell_rect, `0x4632bf` solved_scan; eb `0x47e52f` translate, `0x47f275` follow_pointer, `0x47fa11` rotate_piece, `0x47fb76` multi_pick; ea `0x4841cb` translate, `0x48421e` piece_init, `0x484935` set_layer, `0x484caf` clamp_rect, `0x484d87` follow_pointer, `0x4853b0` rotate_piece, `0x4866b4` inside_area, `0x486725` check_solved; f5 `0x4b832c` clock_update, `0x4b844d` score_update, `0x4b85f6` pick_char, `0x4b866e` pick_position, `0x4b8783` first_wave; 2d `0x4cf0a9` rand_mod, `0x4cf0ce` pick_record. Each acceptance note states its limits (native-only guards such as `BUL_ERR_BOUNDS`, `TQZ_ERR_STUCK`, a zero divisor that errors natively where the original faults; the oracle compares aggregate state, so a function's result is seen through the parent's effects).

Read and not accepted (18), with the fix that would close each: `0x45bda7` score_check (native returns void, original returns 1 or 0), `0x47e8fe` and `0x4846c2` top_piece (one symbol serves two originals; two thin wrappers would do), `0x4809a7`, `0x480a1d`, `0x480abe` sound_blip (one symbol for three originals; `0x480a1d` also calls `0x480b34`), `0x485ca1`, `0x485f12`, `0x485d52` piece_sound / pick_alternate and `0x485fb6`, `0x48633c` caption/feedback (merged or partial), `0x42caa8` hotspot_rects (returns unsupported for a row the original handles; oracle lists it as difference E3), `0x45b568` emit_pair (is a render-plan blit, not the rectangle translator), `0x41632b` avf_player_stop (merges four stops; frees left to the caller), `0x48945b` (skips the `owner+0x20` link store), `0x4cb944` (skips the `+0x136` loop counter), `0x4e7bf3` (omits the six-slot teardown), `0x4b642d` (fragment of the d1 phase machine). Not looked at: the 25 candidates whose only native symbol is in `blackmoor_app.mm` (not compiled in the cloud), the 54 rows with no native symbol citing the VA, and the 78 FL-10 rejects (inlined into larger symbols); the coordinator has FL-12 on the inlined rows.

Full suite on Linux (xcrun stand-in, every build script first, 211 test files run 4 at a time): 2,345 tests, 63 files with failures; the same 63 files re-run on clean `main` (b3e3bce4) give the identical 67 failing test ids (Mac-bound: ASan runtime, AppKit, ffmpeg, `/private/tmp`, oracle import path), 0 new. The 9 new test modules pass (`python3 -m unittest tests.test_fl11_<family>_direct`).

## Handoff: FL-14 executed functions no native symbol cited, 22 more ported (2026-10-18 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **316 ported, 128 not needed, 3,744 pending** (was 294 after FL-13, PR 130). All 22 are `native_oracle_verified`. The 54 executed rows with no native symbol citing the VA were triaged by two Sonnet readers (pseudocode against the native code in the family file). 28 looked one-to-one; the other 26 are inlined into a larger native function or have no native counterpart. For the 28, two Sonnet implementers added the original address to the native symbol's leading comment (comment-only edits in 13 `src/` files; the one pinned hash, `kernel_sha256` in `tests/test_action_family_eb_runtime.py`, was refreshed) and wrote direct C tests (`tests/fl14_x_direct_test.c`, `tests/fl14_y_*_direct_test.c`, wrapped by `tests/test_fl14_x_direct.py` and `tests/test_fl14_y_direct.py`) with expected values from the original pseudocode. Two independent Sonnet readers then re-read all 22 against the pseudocode and the disassembly without seeing the implementers' claims: 22 accepted, 0 rejected. They also built the tests under ASan/UBSan (clean) and one reader ran planted mutants on the four `tqz_*` functions (all caught except two equivalent-looking weak spots below).

Credited: score/time display `0x445f53`, `0x445fc5`, `0x446532`, `0x446548`, `0x44655e`, `0x446598`; f5 `0x4b8087`, `0x4b80a5`, `0x4b8193`, `0x4b82cd`; 7d `0x4eb70f`; invalidate/mark-dirty functions `0x4b5c11` (d1), `0x48d0e5` (d0), `0x45b477` (da), `0x4618ab` (d3), `0x4758e8` (e4), `0x47e440` (eb), `0x4840d9` (ea), `0x48bc38` and `0x48c148` (d8), `0x44d19d` (aa); d3 `0x4618df`.

Limits stated in the acceptance notes: the invalidate stores go through the host `mark_dirty` callback (the 0x5d15a5 table store is not in this C code); native-only guards and codes (`STD_ERR_BOUNDS` for a score outside 0..99999999 or a clock value outside 0..99, where the original stores anything; NULL-argument and host-error returns; `d8_press` skips a `hot` outside 0..3); row phase, candidate and body are parameters instead of globals; `tqz_key` uses the native copy `tqz_key_filter` of the 0x437ea1 filter (tested equal on all 256 bytes). P3 hardening not done: click/key cases with state -1 and phase 0x101 (`(uint8_t)row_phase == 1` mutants survive), `e4r_invalidate` has no NULL check on `mark_dirty`, and the typed-struct families (d1, d0, da, d3, d8, aa) cannot check original byte offsets in the tests because the fields only exist in comments.

Dropped after review, with reasons:
- **Scanner ambiguity (6 rows, native code is fine):** `0x437ea1` pwd_char_accepted (also a second copy, `tqz_key_filter`), `0x4b8520` char_allowed, `0x45be3f` real_name, `0x47e99e` eb rect_valid, `0x47ec06` near_unplaced and `0x484f0d` ea rect_valid. `symbol_area` in the ledger finds two definitions because the helper is called inside an `if (...) {` header (the scan pattern `name(...) {` matches the call). The citation comments are already in the source; each needs the call sites hoisted out of the `if`, or a thin wrapper, plus the mutation-check anchors that pin the call text (for example `tests/action_family_76_runtime_mutation_check.py`, `action_family_eb_runtime_mutation_check.py`). The direct tests for them are in `tests/fl14_x_direct_test.c` and `tests/fl14_y_*_direct_test.c` and pass.
- **Not strictly one-to-one (adapter or plan-only):** `0x48bd91` d8_hit_test, `0x48c0ac` d8_click, `0x48cc70` d8_render_plan, `0x4cf749` d2_render_plan, `0x4eac4c` action79_visit (stores returned in output structs, the caller must apply them), `0x4460d6` and `0x4462d3` std_init_score/clock (slot registration is one host call, native adds an early return), `0x4e7253` action_ui_apply_phone (native-only refusals and caller-owned table), `0x412787` (a47_step differs: kind range check, full-table error, no completion call).
- **Inlined in larger native functions (belong to FL-12, not credited here):** `0x44727a` (action_family28_step), `0x44d9b5` (add_blit), `0x4caf8a` (c1c_visit), `0x4e303b`, `0x4eb96e`, `0x4e7d04` (teardown stays in a host callback nothing implements), `0x4e89eb` (inventory_view_hover), `0x4fba69`.
- **No native counterpart:** `0x4121b8`, `0x415011` (AVF file mapping), `0x423b51`, `0x43f04b`, `0x4477d1`, `0x447ac7` (surface code works on structs and adds checks), `0x493a47` (stub `return 0`), `0x4bedfa` (Bink close), `0x4e7786` (thunk).

## Handoff: FL-15 near-miss rows, 8 more ported (2026-10-18 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **324 ported, 128 not needed, 3,736 pending** (was 316 after FL-14, PR 131). All 8 are `native_oracle_verified`. One Sonnet reader, working from the disassembly first and the native code and tests second, accepted all 8 and ran 35 planted mutants against the direct tests; it asked for one more test case (signed layers in `top_piece_placed`), which is in.

Credited: the six rows FL-14 dropped for scanner ambiguity, `0x437ea1` pwd_char_accepted, `0x4b8520` char_allowed, `0x45be3f` real_name, `0x47e99e` (eb) and `0x484f0d` (ea) rect_valid, `0x47ec06` near_unplaced; and the two FL-13 rows `0x484622` top_piece_placed (ea) and `0x47e9d3` top_piece_47e9d3 (eb).

How the scanner rows were fixed: no native code changed. `symbol_area` in `decomp-progress/function_ledger.py` counted a call such as `if (rect_valid(...)) {` as a second definition. A match now counts as a definition only when an identifier or `*` (a return type or declarator) comes before the name; two real definitions are still "ambiguous" and a call-only symbol is still "not found". Hoisting the calls instead would have changed five hash-pinned kernels and forced oracle re-classification. The rule is covered by `SymbolAreaTests` in `tests/test_function_ledger.py` (fails without the fix); the ledger regenerates identically for the 316 earlier rows. `0x484f0d` also had a receipt blocker (`A-EA.1`, host effects of handler 0x4867bd); it is cleared by `blockers_reviewed` because the function is a pure 53-byte predicate that calls nothing.

How the two oracle rows were fixed: the original `0x484622` is called from the rotate click with the list of pieces whose rotate zone holds the pointer, and the old scenarios never had two. `tests/action_family_ea_runtime_original_oracle.py` gets six `rotate:placed*` scenarios (pre-placed overlapping pieces, layer tables in several orders, pointer inside two or three rotate zones), now executing all 160 bytes. The eb helper `0x47e9d3` only misses its empty-list path, which no click reaches (the native kernel refuses that pick, K3), so a new small oracle `tests/action_family_eb_top_piece_oracle.py` loads the RT-56 machine and calls the originals `0x47e9d3` and `0x47e8fe` directly on forced lists (empty, one, ascending, descending, ties, negative layers) and compares the returns with the oracle's `list_top`. A separate script was chosen so the eb oracle script and its pinned hash and mutant classification stay untouched. Both recorded runs were merged into `function-execution.json` with `record_function_execution.py collect --write --merge` (ea run about 6 min).

Limits: the eb helper's oracle check compares the original with the oracle's model, not native against original in one run; native results for the same inputs come from `fl13_eb_direct_test.c`. The oracle checks of `0x484622` compare the native rotate click, not the return value alone. Reader P3 notes, not fixed: `top_piece`/`top_piece_of_list` have no bound on a corrupt count or list entry (unreachable from valid state); the near_unplaced test cannot kill `>>1` for `/2` or a signed type byte; `ref_437ea1` in the test assumes a signed `char`. The diff reviewer also noted the new `symbol_area` rule would accept a call written as `if (n * f(a)) {` (a `*` before the name) as a definition, and rejects a definition behind a macro such as `EXPORT(int) f(`; neither occurs in the sources, and a rescan of all 147 accepted (path, symbol) pairs changes exactly the six formerly ambiguous ones.

Not credited and why: the inlined rows stay with FL-12 (open PR 129), the 25 `blackmoor_app.mm` rows and the adapter/plan-only rows were out of scope.

Next: the 25 `blackmoor_app.mm` rows or further porting; FL-12 (PR 129) still waits on the inlined-class card.

Verification: `extract_function_evidence.py --check` and `build_function_ledger.py --check` pass; `tests.test_function_ledger` and `tests.test_extract_function_evidence` (371 tests) pass, as do `tests.test_fl13_ea_direct`, `tests.test_fl13_eb_direct`, `tests.test_fl14_x_direct`, `tests.test_fl14_y_direct`, `tests.test_action_family_ea_runtime` and `tests.test_action_family_eb_runtime.Mutation.test_oracle_run_is_current` (the eb oracle script is unchanged). Full suite on Linux (xcrun shim, `/private/tmp` symlink, every build script first, 219 test files run 4 at a time, 900 s per file): 63 files fail on the branch; the same 63 re-run on clean `origin/main` (36daea96) give the same failing test ids (57) and the same exit codes, except `test_action_family_ee_runtime`, which timed out on the branch while three other jobs were competing for the CPU and passes alone in 7 minutes together with the ea and fl13 files. 0 new failures. The slow eb, d3, da, e4 and f5 runtime files time out at 900 s on both. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run.

## Handoff: FL-16 real porting of 36 small executed methods, 36 more ported (2026-10-18 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **360 ported, 128 not needed, 3,700 pending** (was 324 after FL-15, PR 132). All 36 are `native_oracle_verified`. Unlike FL-10 to FL-15 this slice wrote new native code: `src/original_methods_fl16.c` / `.h` hold 36 one-to-one ports of GAME.EXE methods that the recorded oracle runs already execute completely but that no native symbol isolated (the runtime kernels inline them or work on typed structs). Every function is its own symbol (`m<va>_<name>`) with the original address in its leading comment, and works on the original `this` object as raw bytes at the original offsets. Original globals live in `Fl16World`; calls into unported original code, `GetTickCount`, `BinkClose` and the surface manager go through `Fl16Host` callbacks; a call to another function of the file is a direct call.

Ported: getters and setters `0x4f709c`, `0x4c0c18`, `0x4c0c2c`, `0x4c0c54`, `0x4c0c69`, `0x4d3c31`, `0x4ee5cd`, `0x4edf96`, `0x493a47`; resets and stores `0x4234c8`, `0x423000`, `0x423097`, `0x47d77d`, `0x47e25a`, `0x4bedb5`, `0x4bede7`, `0x4bf38c`, `0x4e76d5`, `0x4e76ed`, `0x4e7ba7`, `0x42e560`; helpers `0x434773` (DATA tag), `0x4347b8` (big-endian dword), `0x43f04b` (strided row copy), `0x44d9b5` and `0x45b568` (two copies of the rectangle shift); tick users `0x4d3d20`, `0x4b8e42`; forwarding methods and small sequences `0x4bedfa`, `0x4d3c42`, `0x4e7786`, `0x4faf98`, `0x4ccfbd`, `0x4ccd70`, `0x4e7728`, `0x4e7757`.

Checks: a Sonnet reviewer read all 36 against the disassembly (not only the pseudocode) and ran 125 planted mutants; no P1. Its findings were fixed in the tests: byte-1 sign extension of `0x4347b8`, the full signed word slot of `0x4b8e42`, store-before-call order of the six forwarding methods (the host hooks now snapshot object state), handles/returns with a zero low byte or half, an overlapping copy for `0x43f04b`, and bit 15 of the `0x42e560` sources. A re-run of the survivors shows them killed. `call_4fadc4` takes a `uintptr_t` first argument because the original passes a char pointer. Tests: `tests/fl16_direct_test.c` (36 cases, ASan/UBSan clean) wrapped by `tests/test_fl16_direct.py`.

The ledger evidence also links a few already-ported or pending neighbours (callers and callees named in the new comments, such as `0x475a1e`, `0x43dbd5`, `0x4edfaf`) to the new files by address mention only; that is not a credit change. Limits: nothing is bounds-checked (as in the original; callers own the object); the callbacks stand in for original code that is not ported yet, so the ledger credits each function on the aggregate oracle window plus the direct test, as before. No kernel calls these symbols yet; they are the one-to-one native counterparts the ledger needs.

Not done, with the reason: nine candidates carry receipt blockers (`0x424035`, `0x42405a`, `0x42ca22`, `0x4fba69`, `0x4fc05d`, `0x4cd218`, `0x4e7d04`, `0x4eb96e`, `0x4e303b`) and would need a written `blockers_reviewed` rationale; larger executed rows (`0x4e444d` byte-string lookup, `0x423259` hash lookup, `0x4c7481`, `0x4d3836`, `0x4ccdab`, `0x4b7f3b`) need their own read. FL-12 (PR 129) still owns the inlined rows.

Next: the nine blocked rows (review the receipts), then the mid-size executed rows above.

## Handoff: FL-17 real porting of 27 more small executed methods, 27 more ported (2026-10-18 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **387 ported, 128 not needed, 3,673 pending** (was 360 after FL-16, PR 133). All 27 are `native_oracle_verified`. Like FL-16 this slice wrote new one-to-one native code: `src/original_methods_fl17.c` / `.h` hold 27 ports of GAME.EXE methods whose recorded oracle runs execute every byte (`function-execution.json`) but that no native symbol isolated. Each is its own symbol (`m<va>_<name>`) citing the original address in its leading comment, works on the original `this` or stack-argument object as raw bytes at the original offsets, and keeps the original store and call order. New against FL-16: a dword inside an object that holds a pointer is an original 32-bit address read through `Fl17Host.resolve`; globals that hold pointers are native pointers in `Fl17World`; the FL-16 methods that these call (`0x4ccfbd`, `0x4d3c31`, `0x4d3d20`) are called directly through `Fl17Host.fl16`; `fl16` and `resolve` are required fields when those functions run.

Ported: the nine FL-16 leftovers with receipt blockers `0x424035`, `0x42405a`, `0x42ca22`, `0x4fba69`, `0x4fc05d`, `0x4cd218`, `0x4e7d04`, `0x4eb96e`, `0x4e303b`; plus `0x4d36ed`, `0x4caf8a`, `0x4cf749`, `0x48c0ac`, `0x415011`, `0x4eac4c` (blocker A-79.1), the six cleanups `0x48d067`, `0x461827`, `0x4b5b8a`, `0x45b3ea`, `0x483f6a`, `0x44d107`, the five begin-scene wrappers `0x44d154`, `0x45b42e`, `0x461862`, `0x48d09c`, `0x4b5bc8`, and `0x47591f`.

Blockers: 10 acceptances carry `blockers_reviewed` rationales (each at least 40 characters; read from the handoff JSONs). RT-16, RT-4, RT-3, RT-14 and RT-11 concern callers, producers or consumers of the method, not its body. A-33.1, A-2D.1, A-6D.1 and A-79.1 name the function (or its callee) as unverified in the slice's static pass; the rationale there rests on the later execution census (all bytes executed) plus the direct test, and says what stays unclaimed (the handler, dispatcher arm and host callbacks).

Checks: a Sonnet `slice-reviewer` read all 27 against the disassembly and ran about 800 mutants: no P1. Its P2s were test gaps and are fixed: call-order of `0x4eac4c` (the 0x4ea06c stand-in now sees the global and record byte as they were and can change the global), flag-store order in `0x4e303b`/`0x4eb96e`, signed word indexes in `0x48c0ac`/`0x4eac4c` (record -1 sits in slack below the records), and handle values in every byte lane for `0x415011`; P3 width cases were widened and the header documents the required host fields and the negative-index slack. The listed survivors were re-run and are killed (the remaining one, swapping two independent stores in `0x4e303b`, is equivalent). Tests: `tests/fl17_direct_test.c` (27 cases, ASan/UBSan clean) wrapped by `tests/test_fl17_direct.py`.

Limits: as in FL-16 the credit rests on the execution census (the original bytes ran in a recorded oracle run) plus implementer-written fixtures; no test runs the original bytes on the same fixtures as the native code (a Unicorn differential run for `0x42ca22`, `0x48c0ac`, `0x415011`, `0x4eac4c` would close that, Unicorn is not installed in the cloud box). Nothing is bounds-checked, as in the original; world memory is separate from object images, so a negative index that lands on another global in the original is not mirrored. No kernel calls these symbols yet. FL-12 (PR 129) still owns the inlined rows.

Not done: the larger executed candidates `0x4e444d`, `0x423259`, `0x4c7481`, `0x4d3836`, `0x4ccdab`, `0x4b7f3b` and small rows that are only partly executed (not eligible); `0x4e7bf3`, `0x4121b8`, `0x4b7051`, `0x4c924d` are fully executed with no blockers and are the next easy ones.

Next: the mid-size executed rows above, then Track M data if Glib answers its card.

Verification: `extract_function_evidence.py --check` and `build_function_ledger.py --check` pass; `tests.test_function_ledger`, `tests.test_extract_function_evidence`, `tests.test_fl16_direct` and `tests.test_fl17_direct` (436 tests) pass. Full suite on Linux (xcrun shim, `/private/tmp` symlink, every build script first, 221 test files run 4 at a time, 900 s per file): 69 files fail on the branch (63 exit 1, 6 timeouts); the same 69 files re-run on clean `origin/main` (64b8ea08) give the identical failing test ids and exit codes, except `test_action_family_ee_runtime`, which timed out on the branch while the reviewer was running alongside and passes alone (8 tests, 335 s). 0 new. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run.

## Handoff: FL-18 Unicorn differential check and 21 more ported methods (2026-10-18 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **408 ported, 128 not needed, 3,652 pending** (was 387 after FL-17, PR 134). All 21 new rows are `native_oracle_verified`.

Differential check (new): `tests/original_differential.py` maps the PE image from `work/original/GAME.EXE` into Unicorn (in memory only, no original bytes are committed), runs one original function on random fixtures, replaces its callees with recording stubs, and compares memory, the return value, the bytes popped by `ret N` and the ordered callee log against the native function (through `tests/fl_differential_shim.c`) on the same inputs. Every instruction of the original must be reached over the scenarios. Pointers are mapped back to guest addresses, pointers to locals compare as "L", and word arguments are masked to 16 bits. `tests/test_fl_differential.py` covers the four FL-17 suggestions (`0x42ca22`, `0x48c0ac`, `0x415011`, `0x4eac4c`) and six FL-16 ports (`0x44d9b5`, `0x45b568`, `0x4b8e42` and three more); `tests/test_fl18_differential.py` covers all 21 new ports. All 31 cases pass, and a mutant run on the FL-17 ports and the FL-18 ports is caught (the few survivors the reviewer listed are noted below). The ten FL-16/17 acceptances list the differential test. Re-run: `python3 -m unittest tests.test_fl_differential tests.test_fl18_differential` (needs `unicorn` and `capstone` from pip and `work/original/GAME.EXE`; the tests skip nothing, they fail without the image).

Ported (new, `src/original_methods_fl18.c` / `.h`, direct tests `tests/fl18_direct_test.c` via `tests/test_fl18_direct.py`): `0x4c924d`, `0x4121b8`, `0x4e7bf3`, `0x4b7051`, `0x4b7f3b`, `0x4b7ff4`, `0x4e7253`, `0x4e8dc8`, `0x45b7a8`, `0x4b81ff`, `0x423b51`, `0x4cc79a`, `0x4b6097`, `0x4b8d5e`, `0x410f99`, `0x44d624`, `0x4631d1`, `0x41632b`, `0x4c08d3`, `0x4c0774`, `0x4d07e7`. Calls into original code that is not ported go through one callback, `Fl18Host.call(callee, this, args, nargs)` (args[0] is the last-pushed argument); fixed original tables are passed by guest address. None of the 21 had a receipt blocker, so no `blockers_reviewed` text was needed.

Not done: the larger candidates `0x4e444d`, `0x423259`, `0x4c7481`, `0x4d3836`, `0x4ccdab` (each needs a contained change beyond one-to-one, or has calls into un-ported code that would need new fixtures); the 16 rows PR 129 (FL-12) owns were left alone. Rows with only partly executed bodies stay ineligible.

Checks: a Sonnet `slice-reviewer` disassembled all 21 originals against the ports: no P1. Fixed from its findings: the `0x4b8d5e` limit sign extension and the `0x4b81ff` bottom edge are now covered by the differential (the mutants die), the header comment about `0x4b7f3b` and `0x4d36ed` is corrected, the `reviewer` field no longer reads as self-review, and three shortened FL-16 notes got their dropped sentence back. Known limits: GetTickCount is a silent constant in the differential, so a port that calls it twice is not caught; signed-word wraps above 0x8000 are not reached in `0x4e7253` and `0x4cc79a`; "every instruction reached" is union coverage over the scenarios, not per-branch combinations; callee-saved registers are not compared; the credit is still not a claim about original source structure, and no kernel calls these symbols yet. `Fl17World` and `Fl18World` both hold `value_5c39e5` and `records_5bb5e5`, so whoever wires them must keep one copy.

Verification: `extract_function_evidence.py --check` and `build_function_ledger.py --check` pass; the ledger, evidence, FL-16/17/18 direct and differential tests pass. Full suite on Linux (xcrun shim that maps the macOS clang flags, `/private/tmp` symlink, every build script first, 225 test files run 4 at a time, 900 s per file): 64 files fail on the branch (6 timeouts); the same 64 on clean `origin/main` (aa55b8ba) are compared in the PR description. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run.

Next: the larger executed rows above, then Track M data if Glib answers its card.

Blockers cleared through `blockers_reviewed` (each rationale says the receipt item only listed the function as unmodelled and the port covers exactly that function): `0x4ceb3b`, `0x4cecf7`, `0x4ea269`, `0x4e444d`, `0x4ccff5` (two), `0x44d597` (the writer 0x44d604 is inside it; the writer 0x4574cd and the 0x43b1d7..0x43bfb6 body stay pending).

Skipped: `0x423f57` ported and tested (`m423f57_load_entry` is in fl19a) but NOT credited: it has 36 receipt blockers and one RT-5 divergence that need one-by-one review; `0x4477d1` (64x64 `_ftol` x87 loop, `__chkstk` 0x40d0 frame, double stack argument) is not ported; it can now follow the `0x4fca60` approach. The 16 inlined rows of PR 129 (FL-12) were not touched. Larger executed rows still pending: `0x48d5f4`, `0x48cc70`, `0x4876ed`, `0x44d28c`, `0x47608e`, `0x4b8a27`, `0x4b9a88`, `0x4cb586`, `0x4619be`, `0x481cb9`, `0x463b10`, `0x412db8`, `0x42caa8` and bigger.

Checks: two Sonnet `slice-reviewer` runs (one per batch) disassembled every original against its port: no P1. Fixed from them: `0x423259` now uses one contiguous 0x48-byte frame like the original (a 36 to 39 character name or node text ran past the old separate 0x24 arrays; the differential now draws such names), `0x4b617a` and `0x48bd91` resolve the record only after the early returns, `0x434630` copies forward byte by byte, `0x4fca60` builds with `-ffp-contract=off` (pragma for clang, flag in the test builds; a fused multiply-add changes a result), and the header states the host contract of the float-to-int routine `0x50ce04`. Scratch mutants (124 for batch a, 8 to 23 per function for batch b) were all caught except equivalent ones (`& 31` on x86-masked shifts, a dead return).

Known limits: the differential compares memory only at the end of a run, not at the moment of each host call, so a port that stores after a callee that reads that state would not be caught (the reviewers read the order by hand: stores and calls are in the original order); `0x4fca60` computes the x87 with C doubles, bit-exact over thousands of scenarios under the CRT control word 0x27f (static evidence: no `fldcw` outside the CRT; nothing proves a Bink or DirectDraw DLL leaves it alone at run time); the CRT string predicates and the float-to-int routine are host calls; the unbounded copies in `0x412787`, `0x434630`, `0x4460d6`, `0x4462d3` are as unbounded as the originals; the credit is not a claim about original source structure and no kernel calls these symbols yet.

Verification: `extract_function_evidence.py --check` and `build_function_ledger.py --check` pass. Full suite on Linux (same shims as FL-18: xcrun wrapper, `/private/tmp` link, every build script first, test files 4 at a time, 900 s per file): 228 test files on the branch: 62 fail (6 timeouts); the same 61 fail on clean `origin/main` (357efceb) and `test_action_family_ee_runtime`, which timed out under load on the branch, passes alone (346 s) and on main, so there is no failure that main does not also have. The first pass also showed that the xcrun wrapper must map `-dynamiclib` to `-shared`. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run.

Documented, not changed: 0x47ff0e leaves a half-finished transition when it rejects a caption longer than 200 (the original overruns its frame there; the host must treat code 1 as "dropped" and clear the fields), no bounds checks on counts and table indices (callers must keep them in range), batch b mutation runs are scratch scripts that are not committed and no batch b sanitizer test is committed (ASan/UBSan was run by the worker and the reviewer at -O1 and -O2).

Word arguments are compared by their low 16 bits as in earlier slices. No kernel calls these symbols yet and nothing in the macOS glue was built or run.

Ledger notes: acceptance `note` text is limited to 240 characters and `reviewer` to 80 by `decomp-progress/function_ledger.py`; the evidence extractor needs each C direct test to name its symbol inside the test function (a `(void)symbol;` line when the test goes through a runner helper).

Review: one Sonnet `slice-reviewer` disassembled all 23 originals against the ports: no P1. Callees that change `self` are not exercised (stubs change nothing); the ports re-read fields as the original does. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run, and no kernel calls these symbols yet.

Direct C tests must name the symbol inside the test function (batch b's table runner got one wrapper per function for that reason). ## Handoff: FL-12 class for inlined original functions (2026-10-19 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger counts do not change: **546 ported, 128 not needed, 3,514 pending** (main after PR 142). Glib answered Yes (2026-10-19, 10:57 AM PDT) on counting inlined functions as ported.

What this PR adds: the ledger class `native_oracle_verified_inlined` (`decomp-progress/function_ledger.py`; tests in `tests/test_function_ledger.py`; AGENTS.md "Function ledger"; the progress template shows it in the split line; `annotate_machine.py` treats it as a native counterpart). Rule: game-region functions of game origin only; a recorded oracle run executed every byte and the oracle drives the enclosing native symbol (named in the oracle script or its test driver); the acceptance note is 80+ characters and names that symbol; library, CRT, MFC and Vorbis rules never count.

Why no new acceptances: FL-12 reviewed 155 executed-but-uncredited rows and accepted 46 (20 inlined, 26 one-to-one) on 2026-10-18. By the time main was merged, FL-14, FL-17 and FL-20 had already accepted all 46 as `native_oracle_verified`, with the same native symbols, so crediting them again would add nothing. The 20 inlined rows therefore stay in `native_oracle_verified`; no function was moved or re-reviewed. The class is ready for the next slice that needs it (a symbol that contains the original's logic but is not one-to-one).

Left as is: the rows the readers rejected (pointer, click and render lookups that need the lookup and cursor cells moved into the native symbol and compared by the oracle; `0x4e444d`, `0x4c0774` need the oracle to drive the symbol). The citation comments proposed for the kernel files were dropped: they broke the eb source hash pin and the f5 mutation anchor.

Verification: `extract_function_evidence.py --write`, `build_function_ledger.py --write` and `tests.test_function_ledger` (239 tests) pass after merging main. `src/` is identical to main, so the runtime test files were not rerun. Not verified: `FunctionLedgerTests` in `decomp-progress/test_progress.py` (fails on main too because of the stale playable status), so the template change is only checked by reading.

- batch a, `src/original_methods_fl26a.c` / `.h`: `0x4d3073` (`m4d3073_session_tick`, switch on `[0x583204]-2`, four arms plus the `ja` default), `0x469057`, `0x4b42d3`, `0x4897cd` (`m<va>_hit_test`).
- batch b, `src/original_methods_fl26b.c` / `.h`: `0x423fbb`, `0x4eb107`, `0x42406d`, `0x424201`, `0x42ea87`, `0x4eb184`.
- batch c, `src/original_methods_fl26c.c` / `.h`: `0x424346` (`m424346_node_condition`, every kind arm and both jump tables `0x424a3c`, `0x424a98`).
- Dropped, back to the pool: `0x4e0978` (four instructions at `0x4e0df7..0x4e0e04` sit behind `mov ecx,1; test ecx,ecx; je 0x4e0df7`, the only reference to that address, so no input reaches them and N/N cannot be met; its blocker RT-22 at `0x4e0a22` is not reviewed). Not attempted: `0x4b5d17`, `0x47b767` (dead-code guards, FL-25), `0x43a7ca` (10,845 bytes) and `0x4f0059` (9,655 bytes). Worker notes: `tasks/fl26a-handoff.md`, `fl26b-handoff.md`, `fl26c-handoff.md` (hashes, per-function oracle digests, hooks, mutation triage).

Oracle: `tests/fl26<x>_original_oracle.py` run only original bytes through `tests/fl26<x>_fixtures.py`; N/N for all 11 (`0x4d3073` 44/44, `0x469057` 111/111, `0x4b42d3` 129/129, `0x4897cd` 125/125, `0x423fbb` 22/22, `0x4eb107` 31/31, `0x42406d` 125/125, `0x424201` 98/98, `0x42ea87` 163/163, `0x4eb184` 329/329, `0x424346` 451/451). One untaken jcc edge, allowlisted with a reason: `jne 0x424305` at `0x424271` in `0x424201` (a node of kind other than 1 or 2 makes the original loop forever; the differential shows the original hits the instruction limit). Blockers of seven rows were cleared with `blockers_reviewed` (RT-4, RT-6, RT-14, A-2E.1 items, each read at the located address); the RT-4 divergence on `0x4eb107` and `0x4eb184` is a divergence, not a blocker, and stays.

Review: three Sonnet `slice-reviewer` passes (a+b, c; the b fixes were not re-reviewed): no P1, no divergence on valid input. Fixed: `0x4eb184` now models the original frame (60-byte buffer up to the live flag; translated text up to 0x3c characters and record or row strings up to 0x7b are accepted as the original does; a string of 0x7c characters, where the NUL becomes the low byte of the live flag, and a structure string of 0x38 or more, which reaches saved ebp, are rejected natively); the `0x4eb184` oracle window now ends at `0x4eb5f2`; kind `0x14` of `0x424346` checks its reads and stores before any host call; first-hit-wins profiles for `0x469057`, `0x4b42d3` and both loops of `0x4897cd`; headers state that table indices, record numbers and the `0x4897cd` count (at most 40) are caller-validated. Mutation tools: a 136 mutants (2 equivalent survivors), b 220 (12), c 210 (6); every survivor judged equivalent by reading.

Divergences and unverified branches (details in the handoffs): `m42406d` rejects as endless a valid acyclic chain of about 1,025 nodes or more where the flag has to propagate backwards one node per pass (its step limit counts passes across restarts); `0x424201` rejects an endless loop the original never leaves; callees that change `self`, a node or a re-read global are not exercised; `0x4eb184` passes host pointers (`local`, `buf`, a row of `self`) to `0x4fb77a`, `0x448d11` and `0x423259` but original addresses elsewhere, so the integration step must say which side translates them and the structure sizes (0x34, 0x3c); the `resolve` host call is trusted for the full access width; `0x424201` still stubs `0x424346` by address, swapping in `m424346_node_condition` is an integration step. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run and no kernel calls these symbols yet.

Next pool: game pending rows 2,129. `executed_partial` windows still pending: session input and navigation 5 (`0x4e0978`, `0x4b5d17`, `0x47b767` with dead-code guards that no fixture can pass, and the two large dispatchers `0x43a7ca` and `0x4f0059`), then `action_family_35_runtime` 28, then a1, 3f, 35 bink, 3e, 2d. Open for Glib: a dead-code rule for functions like `0x4e0978`, `0x4b5d17` and `0x47b767` (credit them with the unreachable block listed, or leave pending).

Verification (FL-26): `extract_function_evidence.py --check`, `build_function_ledger.py --check`, `tests.test_function_ledger`, `tests.test_extract_function_evidence` and the three FL-26 direct and differential module pairs pass on the final tree (after merging main with PR 129, which changed the ledger generator but not `src/`). Full suite on Linux (xcrun wrapper that drops `-arch`/`-framework` and maps `-dynamiclib` to `-shared`, `/private/tmp` as a link to `/tmp`, every build script first, 265 test files 4 at a time, 900 s per file) on the branch before that merge: 62 files fail. The same 62 files on clean `origin/main` (aa19355f) fail with identical exit codes, except `test_action_family_f5_runtime`, which timed out under suite load on the branch and passes alone on the branch (670 s) and on main. Net: 0 new failures. Map: `/mnt/project-files/progress-map-after-fl26.png` (ledger panel 557; playable panel seeded, 22 scenes).

## Handoff: FL-27 action family 0x35 runtime rows (2026-10-19 PDT, cloud thread)

Ledger: **585 ported, 128 not needed, 3,475 pending** (was 557 after FL-26, PR 143; PR 144 changed nothing in the ledger). 28 new `native_oracle_verified` rows (game 406 of 2,507, pending game rows 2,101). Same recipe as FL-26: a per-batch fixtures module, an original-only oracle script (N/N instructions, both directions of every conditional jump), a one-to-one native port, a direct test and a Unicorn differential. Packet `tasks/FL-27-action-family-35-runtime.md`; three Sonnet implementer passes in their own worktrees (a, b, c), one Sonnet diff review per pass, review fixes applied, oracle runs recorded with `record_function_execution.py run --only fl27` then `collect --merge --write` (149 runs), the 28 acceptances appended to `function-ledger-overrides.json` (notes shortened to the ledger's 240-character limit).

Ported, each one native function citing its original address (handoffs `tasks/fl27a-handoff.md`, `fl27b-handoff.md`, `fl27c-handoff.md` carry hashes, per-function oracle digests, hooks and mutation triage):
- batch a, `src/original_methods_fl27a.c` / `.h` (15): `0x4de1f5`, `0x4de214`, `0x449d90`, `0x44c479` (`m44c479_wait_state`), `0x4bee2c`, `0x4bef85`, `0x4bee5f`, `0x4befc8`, `0x4bef11`, `0x4bf098`, `0x4bf315`, `0x4c2dfb`, `0x4159db`, `0x4bf10f`, `0x4c0c7d` (`m4c0c7d_movie_thread`).
- batch b, `fl27b` (9): `0x449bff`, `0x449a94`, `0x4bf773`, `0x4161d1`, `0x4c2c3a`, `0x4c0d3f`, `0x4c5377`, `0x4c04e3`, `0x415db9`.
- batch c, `fl27c` (4): `0x4497a0`, `0x414ceb`, `0x4c2e97`, `0x44c4c1`.
- Pending, deliberately: `0x4de2d8` (ported and tested, acceptance withheld: its A-51 receipt blockers are about the renderer and UI callbacks it calls, which no oracle here models), `0x4bf9bb` (2,122 bytes) and `0x413187` (4,873 bytes) not attempted.

Oracle: N/N for all 28 (largest `0x44c4c1` 329/329, `0x4c2e97` 298/298, `0x415db9` 202/202). Untaken jump directions, all allowlisted with a reason: `0x4c0c7d` (`mov ecx,1; test ecx,ecx; je 0x4c0d32`: the target is also reached by the `jmp` at `0x4c0ca5`, so all 55 instructions run; credited, and Glib's dead-code ruling is about blocks no input reaches), `0x449a94` (two edges: a consecutive re-test of the same dword, and a path to a null store).

Review: three Sonnet `slice-reviewer` passes, no P1. Fixed: a wait that ends during the limit-th `Sleep` is honoured by `0x44c479` and `0x4c0c7d` (with a direct test); the `0x4c5377` S05-2 blocker rationale named the wrong item; `0x4c2e97`'s zero-mask rejection returned a value its caller reads as success (now 0); header and import-name fixes.

Divergences and unverified branches (details in the handoffs): native rejections replace infinite loops, null stores and 4 GB copies (`0x44c479`, `0x4c0c7d` spin limit, `0x449bff`, `0x449a94`, `0x4c5377`, `0x415db9`, `0x4497a0`, `0x414ceb`, `0x4c2e97`); `0x44c479` sets only `ax`; callees are host stubs, so their own effects are not verified (`0x4bf38c`, `0x4bede7`, `0x415011`, `0x4c4067`, `0x4c5d4b`); `0x4c5377` S05 and S05-2 blockers cleared by `blockers_reviewed` (pixel loop checked against a scripted lock descriptor; the UI lifecycle that calls it and the real lock stay open); `0x4c0d3f` passes host pointers as the r1/r2 arguments of `0x4c5377`, so wiring them needs the integration step to say which side translates; `0x4497a0` does not model its exception frame and only clears `[self+0x36]` (never swaps in another object) at its re-reads; `0x44c4c1` x87 assumes control word 0x27f (53-bit) and the fixtures do not discriminate against 0x37f at realistic frame rates; `0x414ceb` returns 0 where the original leaves `eax = arg`. Nothing in the macOS glue was built or run and no kernel calls these symbols yet.

Next pool: game pending rows 2,101. `executed_partial` windows still pending: session input and navigation (`0x4e0978`, `0x4b5d17`, `0x47b767` dead-code guards; `0x43a7ca`, `0x4f0059` large dispatchers), the rest of `action_family_35_runtime` (`0x4de2d8`, `0x4bf9bb`, `0x413187`), then a1, 3f, 35 bink, 3e, 2d.
